The empire, long divided, must unite; long united, must

divide. Thus it has ever been.

— Luo Guanzhong, Three Kingdoms

The Realm is the jewel at the center of the
world. The queens and princes of Creation bend
their heads to the might of the Dragon-Blooded
host. There is nowhere the traders and armies of the
Realm.do. not travel, and there is no lord who can
count himself safe from the wrath of the children of
the Elemental Dragons. As rulers and masters of the
world, ‘the Dragon-Blooded brought culture, safety
and commerce to the four corners of Creation, pav-

First Age, preserving the vanished glory of that lost
time against the tides of history.

With its ageless sorcerer-queen and its arsenal of
First Age weapons, the empire of the Dragon-Blooded
was, for centuries, an invincible colossus. Now, with
the Empress gone, the juggernaut of empire seems
poised to tear itself 2 apart. The Scarlet Empress’ long

<!--PAGE 11-->
Into this chaos are thrust the players’ characters,
Dragon-Blooded recently graduated from secondary
school. They are the doers and agents of their houses,
pursuing new ventures where the house elders may
not have looked and serving the houses’ interest as
their relatives order them. The world they have grown
up in the arms of is tearing itself apart. All around
them, the Great Houses are devouring the empire to
fuel their rivalry, and though the elders say they are
united against the enemies of the Realm, they squabble
like children and cost the empire satrapies through
indecision and treachery. .

How do these Exalts, raised with the Princedom of
the Earth as their birthright, react to this time of
tumult? Do they seek to further their family and house
or the oft-forgotten greater good of the empire? Do
they cross swords with their oath-sworn brothers at the
behest of warring families, or do they cling to the ideals
of an empire that have come to seem increasingly false?
Will those who stay seek reform or revolution? Will
they seek to further their cause through politics, through
the power of the Philosophy or through military might?
Will those who turn against the Realm linger on the
shores of the Blessed Isle, or will they flee into the
Scavenger Lands, to join up with the forces of Lookshy
or other outcaste groups?

All of these conflicts combine to make the Realm
arich setting. Characters have external enemies, in the
shape of the various Anathema and the Fair Folk, and
internal enemies, in the form of the tensions between
the Realm’s power-holders. Now the Dragon-Bloods
must fulfill their destinies as the greatest warriors the
world has ever seen. Further, their loyalties to their
friends, to their families and to their empire will all be
sorely tested. Each will have to make many terrible
choices in the course of the years to come, and their
hearts and souls, as well as their sword-arms, will be
tested in the balance of crisis. As the world groans

through this vast turning point, these heroes will forge
legends that will be recounted for a thousand years —
or else wiped away in the fire of war and apocalypse.

How To Use Tuis Book

This book contains everything you need to run a
game focusing om the Dragon-Blooded or to include a
very detailed treatment of the Dragon-Blooded in
another Exalted game. In structure, it is similar to the
main Exalted rulebook, except that there are no
Systems, Drama or Antagonists chapters. The contents are as follows:

Introduction:

The section you’re reading now. It contains a
statement of the book’s overall character and a
short glossary.

Chapter One: Setting

This chapter details the Realm, primarily from the
perspective of the Dynasty. It describes the history of
the Realm, the shape of its government and the details
of some of its prominent prefectures.

Chapter Two: The Dragon-Blooded

This chapter is companion material to Chapter
One, examining Dynastic culture and the Great Houses
and, also, the Dragon-Blooded schooling process and
their attitudes toward magicians.

Chapter Three: Character Creation

This chapter contains the rules for creating
Dragon-Blooded characters for use in an Exalted
game. Though they are mechanically compatible
with the Solar Exalted, Dragon-Blooded characters
are not balanced with their Solar counterparts. Storytellers should think about the matter carefully
before trying mixed-Exalt games.

Chapter Four: Traits

This chapter describes the specialized Traits
used in Dragon-Blooded character creation. Traits
detailed in the Exalted book are not discussed here.
The chaprer also contains the few mechanical systems unique to this book.

Chapter Five: Charms
“This chapter details the elemental Charms commonly used ‘by the Dragon- Blooded.

### Lexicon


Most of the terminology in this book was introduced in the Exalted book, and we have not
reproduced that book’s glossary in full. The following entries are either altered to reflect the
Dragon-Blooded point of view or specialized vocabulary relevant to the Dragon-Blooded.

The All-Seeing Eye: The imperial secret police,
who guard the throne against sedition, corruption and
treason. The All-Seeing Eye employs countless mortal
and Dragon-Blocded agents recruited from the Thousand
Scales, but its core are spies and assassins of both the
Bronze Sidereal faction and House Iselsi.

The Blessed Isle: The island at the center of
Creation, which the Scarlet Empress governs directly.
This isle is traditionally the center of the world — it
was the seat of the Solar Deliberative in the days
before the Usurpation and of the Shogunate in the
days before the Contagion.

The Cloister of Wisdom: The secondary school
oriented toward preparing students for service in the
Immaculate Order.

Dominion: A very large prefecture created from
the personal lands of the Shogun in the post-Contagion era. Managing the dominions is a very important
task. There are two dominions, Arjuf and Lord’s Crossing. Juche Prefecture is almost a dominion and is
considered of equivalent stature.

Dragon-Blooded: Spiritually advanced humans
Exalted into power by the blood and might of the Five
Elemental Dragons. The Dragon-Blooded are creatures
of great mystical refinement and are permitted to pray
outside of holy days and to interact with gods and
ghosts. They are the natural-born rulers of Creation.

The Dynasty: The Great Houses and their immediately subordinate lines together comprise the Dynasty,
the ruling elite of the Realm. Dragon-Blooded born
outside the Dynasty are either adopted or married into
its ranks, placed into the legions or the Immaculate
Order, or branded outcastes.

Great House: A line of Dragon-Blooded, generally
descended from the Scarlet Empress, that has special
legal status in the Realm. The Great Houses receive
special tax breaks, provide the Realm’s most important
officers, and officials, lease land at favorable rates from

The Host of the Dragon-Blooded: A more formal
term for the Dynasty.

The House of Bells: A secondary school that prepares young officers for service in the legions.

Imperial Service: The proper name for the imperial bureaucracy, more commonly called the
Thousand Scales.

Itinerant: A wandering Immaculate with special
authority to look into matters of interest to the faith.
Similar to the imperial magistrates.

Lease: The Scarlet Empress owns all the real estate
on the Blessed Isle, and all land deals are trading in
negotiable land-lease agreements issued by the state.
Most peasants on the Blessed Isle pay land-lease fees as
their primary tax, and the Great Houses always have
various leases up for renewal that the Empress can
change the terms of as a form of leverage.

Magistrate: Plenipotentiary officials who speak
with the voice of the Scarlet Empress and act with her
authority. The magistrates investigate corruption
and treason as well as serious criminal cases, and
there is a great degree of competition between them
and the All-Seeing Eye.

The Northern Isles: The two small isles to the
northwest of the Blessed Isle. These islands are “part” of
the Blessed Isle and are governed by prefectural rather
than satrapial methods.

Prefect: The appointee who runs a prefecture.
Prefects are rarely Dragon-Blooded because the job isn’t
seen as challenging enough to merit the skills of a
Prince of the Earth.

Prefecture: The Blessed Isle’s equivalent of a
satrapy, governed by a prefect. Prefectures were generally much more fairly administered than satrapies.
Their taxes were much more reasonable, and the
many faces of the imperial bureaucracy acted as a
check on local power and guaranteed the peasantry
certain rights and protections. Without the Scarlet
Empress to check the growth of house power, many
prefectural administrations have begun to favor
large leaseholders and: to burden the peasants with
oppressive taxes. “

Primary School A chool with-a a genetal focus

<!--PAGE 13-->
to the First Age and forbid the use of rhe terms “First
Realm” and “Old Realm.”

Satrap: A representative of imperial power in a
tributary state, almost always one of the Dragon-
Blooded. The satrap is assisted by a staff that might be
only a half-dozen imperial bureaucrats or that can
include several hundred unExalted imperial citizens, thousands of local employees and dozens of
Dragon-Blooded. Satraps are powerful individuals,
often commanding private armies and with incredible influence on local government. ;

Satrapy: A tributary state or, sometimes, a number of very small stares lumped together for
administrative convenience. These are the building
blocks of the imperial system. Each satrapy is ruled
by whoever the local ruler is — the Realm is not
concerned with the administration of day-to-day
affairs among its tributaries. The satrap is instead
present to look after imperial political and commercial interests and to oversee tribute payments.

The Scarlet Empress: The queen of the Dragon-
Blooded, who ruled from the Realm’s rebirth to her
disappearance five years ago.

Secondary School: A more advanced academy
than a primary school. Most Dynastic Dragon-
Blooded attend one of the Realm's four prominent
secondary schools, while patricians attend lessprominent institutions.

The Spiral Academy: A secondary school devoted
to teaching students the skills necessary for service in
the Thousand Scales.

The Thousand Mazy Paths: The Scarlet Empress’
strategy of divide-and-rule governance, pitting the
imperial government against itself to prevent any challenges to her power from arising.

The Thousand Scales: The bureaucracy that
governs the Realm and manages the tributary states.
The staff of the Scales is primarily patrician, but
most auditors and senior decision makers are Dragon-
Blooded.

Tribute: In addition to oaths of subjection and
loyalty, the’ Scarlet Empress required tribute’ from the

and their house. It was the business of the magistrates

### Suggested Resources


While the resources suggested in the main book
are obviously still applicable to Dragon-Blooded games,
there are also other books that are specifically useful to
games focusing around the Terrestrial Exalted. While
few of these share Exalted’s highly spiritual fantasy
world, all of them provide good insights into the culture and philosophy of the Realm and its
self-aggrandizing rulers.

The Domination. S.M. Sterling. Baen Books. New
York, NY. ISBN 0671-57794-8.

An alternate history featuring a successful expansionist, slave-taking white state in the south of Africa,
these excellent books are so grim as to verge on unreadable. This series provided much of the inspiration for
the culture of the Dragon-Blooded.

The General, Book I: The Forge. S.M. Sterling and
David Drake. Bean Books. New York, NY. ISBN 0671-
72037-6.

Though properly a science fiction novel, this book
is an excellent look at running and serving in an army
in the pre-modern era. There are more books in the
series, but the first is the best. A must for campaigns
involving the legions.

Three Kingdoms. Luo Guanzhong. Foreign Language Press. Beijing, China. ISBN 7-119-00590-1

One of the all-time great epics, this book tells
of the fall of the Han Dynasty after the Yellow
Turban rebellion and the beginning of the Three
Kingdoms period. While extremely long, this book
should prove of great interest to Storytellers and
players of civil war games.

The Swordswoman. Jessica Amanda Salmonson.
Tor Books. New York, NY.

HIsTORY AND THE EMPRESS

Any history of the empire of the Dragon-Blooded
is, by definition, a history of its Empress. She created
it from the smoldering ashes of the Contagion, sustained it when it was weak and guided it throughout its
history. It has always reflected her will and her vision,
and it has always been the instrument by which she has
imposed both upon the world. While there is no formal
cult of personality within the Realm, there really
doesn’t need to be one. Any inhabitant of the lands
under her sway, from the haughtiest Dragon-Blood to
the lowliest slave, knows that, on a fundamental level,
the Empress and her empire are one and the same.


### The Making of an Empress


Every schoolchild of the Realm —- indeed, much
of Creation — knows how the Scarlet Empress came to
her throneThe tale has been told and retold for seven

<!--PAGE 17-->
. First. Age. Legends told of prior efforts by the Fair Folk to

*Seertes


### The Rallying of the Legions


The Contagion left the surviving legions of the Old
Realm worse than decimated. Reduced to a minuscule
fraction of their original fighting strength, they nonetheléss took to the field with alacrity when the Fair Folk and
their barbarian hordes first appeared. The commanders of
the various legions took counsel and decided to establish
their lines as close to the edges of Creation as possible, the
better to deny the Fair Folk subjugated populations from
which they could feed and draw strength. As a result, the
legions of what would someday be called the Threshold
advanced, and the Blessed Isle was emptied of troops to
support them. Elaborate defenses were constructed on the
Isle itself, but the bulk of the island's fighting soldiers were
put in the field as quickly as possible, ferried across the
Inner Sea by whatever craft could be put into service.

Unfortunately, the generals’ plans required more manpower and organization than the legions had to spare. The
defensive lines they'd drawn were too long, their soldiers
spread too thin, and the cooperation between formations
too perfunctory. The haughty Fair Folk lords punched
through the Dragon-Blooded lines in a dozen places. Even
the intervention of long-hidden Sidereal Exalted on the
legions’ behalf proved a temporary stay at best, as draws
became retreats and retreats became routs. Soon, the armies
of the Dragon-Blooded were tumbling back toward the
shores of the Inner Sea in a full panic, while their leaders
grimly prepared for what they thought would bea last stand.


### A Solitary Deserter


Among those officers attempting to hold off the
inevitable was a young captain of the Ninth Legion of the
personal army of the Shogun of the Realm, a woman of
impeccable family and some wealth, whose ability to rally
her troops in the face of even the most dangerous crisis had
earned her several commendations. Fortunate enough to
possess well-equipped and well-motivated soldiers, she
nevertheless realized the futility of fighting the Fair Folk in
the field. Their numbers were too great, their powers too..terrifying, and the Dragon-Bloaded simply too few.

The key, she became convinced, lay in the relics of the

<!--PAGE 18-->
throughout the Threshold, she and her companions sought
to unseal the entrances to the ancient Manse. After weeks
of frantic attempts to breach its defenses, which proved
proof against the mightiest weapons of her age, she managed to open the gates of the Manse and enter.

‘THE PALACE OF THE ANATHEMA

None living in Creation save perhaps the Scarlet
Empress herself know precisely what transpired that day.
What is known is simple: That the Empress found and bent
to her will the ancient systems and engines that contained
within them the might necessary to drive the Fair Folk
back beyond the walls of Creation and that, within hours,
the armies of the soulless invaders were broken and fleeing.

What is not known is the price that was paid. The
woman who would become Empress entered the Palace of
the Anathema with four companions. None of the four
companions survived. Two were slaughtered by the stillserviceable defenses surrounding the engines ofdestruction.
The third sacrificed his heart’s blood upon a hungry stone
slab so that the hot blood might awaken and feed the
ungentle spirits who tended the venerable defenses. And
the last was given, willingly, as a gift by the Empress to the
guardian of the site, her soul torn screaming from her
broken body as a token of the Empress’ deadly earnest. Alli
had been ready to sacrifice themselves for the sake of the
land when they entered the citadel; only the future Empress was left with the terrible burden of survival.

But with the proper gifts made and the engines of
destruction humming eagerly once more, the lone young
officer took them up with a will. She turned the devices on
the Fair Folk hordes, striking at them with all the powers
at her command. And on that day, none could say which
was louder — the wailing of the Fair Folk as they fled back
into the outer dark or the howling, vicious wind, which
scoured the flesh from their immortal bones.


### The Year of Seven Tigers


In the hours in the wake of the fury that signaled the
end to the Contagion, there was stunned silence. For
days, the only sights on battlefields were stunned soldiers
keeping a dazed sentry against a possible counterattack
and looters half-heartedly stripping the bodies of the
dead.'Rain poured steadily from the heavens, casting a
gray veil‘across all Creation and driving even the greediest corpse¢tobber to higher ground. But for a precious

horrors of the Contagion and what followed were too fresh
for them to risk an over-aggressive pursuit.

Certain others began casting about and wondering
what to make of the world. Even as the forces of the various
Threshold daimyos struggled out of the mire, they were
faced with the spectre of a new age of warfare, as the
shattered Shogunate made conquest a possibility now that
mere survival was no longer an issue.

A handful of the boldest and most ambitious of these
generals and daimyos gauged the others’ relative strengths
and decided that it might be better to work together than
against one another. The oldest, Skri Shanash, invited the
others to her camp at what is today the Lap, to discuss the
future of the lands around the Inner Sea. Quickly, they
drew up plans to carve up both the Blessed Isle and the
lands of the Threshold, with provinces apportioned to
each. Satisfied with the arrangement, at least temporarily,
the so-called Seven Tigers returned to their troops and
began a systematic war of conquest.

"THE Empress’ RESPONSE

Word of the Seven Tigers’ depredations reached the
Empress quickly, as refugees from the Threshold streamed
onto the Blessed Isle with tales of butchery and rapine.
Quickly, a picture of the situation painted itself: armies
methodically reducing cities and fortresses, surviving vessels pressed into service as troop transports and the gathering
of an invasion force clearly meant for the Isle itself. In
coastal cities, defenses built in haste against the Fair Folk
were hastily repaired and augmented, and old men and
young girls were mustered to man them. Messengers were
sent to order the Seven Tigers to desist their actions; the
heads of the messengers were returned across the Inner Sea,
their mouths stuffed with the silken scrolls they'd carried.

With terrible clarity, the Empress saw her choices.
Either turn the weapons she had so recently mastered on
those soldiers who once fought alongside her or allow them
to overrun the Isle and then turn on one another. And in
the forests and the Wyld places, she knew the Fair Folk still
lurked, ready to return if they saw weakness.

Reluctantly, she made the only choice she could. Pale
streamers of fire struck from the heavens at the encampments of the Seven. Tigers, Shapes of water that strode
across the waves like mer shed ships. at harbor, and
screaming winds that lefi:

THE Devit’s BARGAIN

Inthe wake of the Empress’ first triumph over her rivals,
she was approached by an emissary of Chejop Kejak, leader
of the Bronze faction of the Sidereal Exalted. Troubled by
the possibility of a recurrence of the Contagion and dismayed by the collapse into depravity of the Dragon-Blooded
that had allowed such a calamity to occur, he wished to
ensure that such never happened again. The Empress, he
judged, was his best hope for achieving this goal.

The Empress, for her part, recognized the precarious
nature of her situation. Having seized power, she could
never let it go, lest her enemies destroy her. If she used her
power indiscriminately to eliminate those who stood against
her, she’d simply raise new generations of enemies — or
depopulate the Realm of the Dragon-Blooded needed to
defend it against further invasions. And while she, personally, was safe from any sane rival so long as she was the only
one with access to the defenses of the Realm, she was well
aware that very few of her rivals were sane.

Kejak’s rough proposition, then, offered a faint glimmer of hope. She returned the emissary with a promise of
acceptance... so long as Kejak came to her. At first stunned
by her insolence, the Sidereal finally swallowed his pride
and journeyed —alone and on foot — to the ancient fortress
that housed the Celestials’ defenses — and the Empress as
well. He knelt before the gates and stayed there, head
bowed, fora full day. Impressed by this show of humility, and
by the fact that the arrangement was important enough to
Kejak that he would humble himself thus, the Empress
opened the doors of the Palace to him and bade him enter.

What transpired in that meeting can be pieced together from later events and scraps of correspondence long
since destroyed. Kejak, maneuvering skillfully, outlined
his problems, what he thought the Empress’ problems were
and where their interests overlapped. He then proposed a
solution: the resurrection of the Realm, under the Empress’ guidance. He’d get what he wanted, in the form of a
stable central political entity that could impose some kind
of social structure on the Dragon-Blooded, and as for the
Empress, she'd receive the empire itself.

_The Empress’ objection to this plan was simple. Officially reestablishing the Realm would, in her opinion,
ngender'a series of bloody civil wars. And while she, with
pepower< of the ancientsat her disposal, was certain to win

assistance of the Bronze faction and its most;
tool, the Immaculate Order, in establish ig

-someday be called'r the

faith was already in place, namely, that the Dragon-
Blooded themselves were essentially divine and that their
word was law. Using this philosophy to explicitly support
the new Realm (as it had implicitly supported the old
before the Contagion) would serve to pacify the masses
and also offer a sop to the other Dragon-Blooded. If they
bought into Kejak’s plan and supported the empire, they
would be regarded as gods. If they didn’t, they'd rapidly find
themselves outside of the empire and at the mercy of the
Anathema and Fair Folk in the hinterlands. Meanwhile,
the humans of the new Realm would be trained to support
the empire as a matter of faith as well as good business,
ensuring the loyalty of the populace, and successive generations of Dragon-Blooded would grow up with a spiritually
and politically advantageous code of conduct in place.

The risks were many. If the populace rejected the new
interpretation of the faith, then any empire attached to it
would be rejected as well. Ifthe Dragon-Blooded refused to
take the bribe Kejak and the Empress offered, then she'd
find herself ruling over an empty desert or embroiled in an
endless series of revolutions and civil wars. Each possibility
was laid out, discussed, and anticipated.

Ultimately, both Kejak and the Empress needed each
other, and to their credit, they recognized this early on.
With the support of a young and vigorous empire, Kejak’s
religion could flourish, and he would have a suitable perch
from which to carry out his endless quest to shape history.
With the support of a faith that advocated her divine right
torule, the Empress’ new kingdom could quickly be unified
behind her. The alternatives — another Contagion, increased barbarian incursions, civil strife, famine and
desolation — were too terrible to contemplate.

And so, the bargain was struck. Kejak would use his
considerable resources to help the Empress proclaim the
new empire and to support her through its early days. She,
in turn, would grant Kejak imperial sponsorship to his
Immaculate Order, with the caveat that it be dedicated to
upholding the reign of the Dragon-Blooded and, through
them, the Empress. Neither trusted the other, but both
recognized the higher loyalties that would bind each to
keep their end of the bargain.

"THE ]MPERIAL PROCLAMATION

Children of the Blessed Isle, soldiers and priests,
Exalted of the Immaculate Dragons and those who toil
in the dust, hear me. Know that the wars and the
Contagion are over, now and forever. Know that the
Fair Folk have been driven back to the empty places
beyond the walls of the world and that it is my power
that has done so. Know that those who sought unlawful
dominion over the Blessed Isle have been vanquished
and their bones sunk beneath the mire at the bottom of
the hungry seas. You have peace. This is my gift to-you.
I think you are all wise enough to accept it.

Your gift to me shall be obedience. For | am the
Scarlet Empress, she who has laid waste to the Fair
Folk and washed clean the Contagion and she who
has protected the Blessed Isle so that you might live.
Mine is the power and the right to rule, and mine is
the power and the right to destroy all those who
would threaten this land.

Let it be proclaimed, therefore, that the Realm
has risen and that the Scarlet Empress reigns over it
with honor as its shogun and queen. Let it be proclaimed that the subjects of the Realm do fealty to the
Empress and serve the Realm faithfully, for it nurtures and protects them even as the Empress does.
And let the enemies of the Realm tremble, for there is
no power in all Creation that can stand against it.

Kejak’s word, the priests therein counseled the troubled

(often through the actions of itinerant priests, who'd been

### Political Repercussions and the Imperial City


they latched onto her like remoras clinging to a shark,
determined to be carried along by the greater power to
their own advantage.

It was a single day after her proclamation that the

‘Empress received the first offer of marriage from one of her
freshly minted vassals. Couched in terms so flowery that it

was practically incomprehensible, the missive from the
Dragon-Blooded Araka Jeresh of Arjuf was simultaneously
a proposal of marriage, an oath of fealty and a condescending reassurance that Jeresh would be happy to assist the
Empress in upholding the burdens of power, which were
clearly too heavy for her.

Green fire rained from the heavens that day over the
Jeresh compound, harming not a single soul but reducing
the haughty Manse in which Araka dwelt to rubble. Much
humbled, he sent a second missive begging the Empress’
pardon, withdrawing his presumptuous offer of marriage and
begging leave to rebuild. Suitably amused, the Empress took
Jeresh as her lover, and in the wake of this sequence of
events, a steady stream of declarations of loyalty, offers of
marriage, proposals of seduction and priceless gifts flowed to
the Imperial Manse(as it was now known).

A steady stream of supplicants to the Scarlet Throne
soon made their way in person to the site of the Imperial
Manse itself, determined to present their persons and
petitions to the Empress directly. To impress her, many of
these petitioners brought rich gifts and large entourages
and camped out in splendid pavilions outside of her
Manse. As the Empress herself was fickle in whom she
chose to see (initially, she took great delight in summoning the humble to her while the wealthy and powerful
waited furiously, but this game soon lost its appeal), some
supplicants waited weeks, months or even longer. Few,
however, dared to depart once they had set up camp and
announced themselves. To do so would be to level a grave
insult at the Empress, and none were willing to do so.

Instead, the encampments of the petitioners became
dotted by permanent dwellings. To be certain, there were
a great many buildings already in place around the Imperial Manse, relics of the First Age that were still quite
habitable. Among these cyclopean structures, those who

sought the Em ress’-favor erected houses for themselves

a result. And as the settlement of petitioners grew, it
became less and less a place to wait to see the Empress and
more and more a place to see and be seen.

Little by little, the Imperial City as it is known today
arose. The Imperial City — for it needed no other name-—
was the place to be if one wished to play the games of
politics. The bulk of the most powerful Dragon-Blooded in
the Realm had residences here. The finest restaurants and
shops, the wisest and most puissant priests, the best and
best-armed legions — all could be found here. And all of
them orbited the Empress and her elegantly simple Manse,
a Manse already beginning to disappear into the outbuildings the acolytes of Pasiap built around it to house the
growing imperial staff.


### The Imperial Service


As the storm of construction swirled around her
dwelling, the Empress (assisted by Jeresh, who, once stripped
of his delusions, had become an extremely competent
advisor) looked past the encampments to the Realm as a
whole. An informal structure of runners, courtiers and
constables had sprung up around the so-called “Imperial
Encampment” to facilitate petitions, resolve squabbles
and, generally, keep the situation in line, and the Empress
recognized that something similar — but better organized
— was needed for the Realm as a whole.


### The All-seeing Eye


The first branch of what would later be called the
Thousand Scales to be organized was the All-Seeing Eye,
which was given charterin RY 12. Initially, ic was nothing
more than a network of informers among the petitioners,
information brokers with something to sell that the Empress might find interesting. However, as more and more
of her more powerful subjects began pressing in on the
Imperial Manse, the Empress felt the need for more and
better intelligence. Even with the powers at her disposal,
her eye could not be cast everywhere at once. There were
too many opportunities for conspiracies that would threaten
the fledgling Realm, too many chances for traitors to
conspire. And so, by means of bribes, threats and cajoling,
she pressed those who had brought information to her into
her.service. Jeresh was installed as the head of the newly
minted All-Seeing Eye, charged with recruiting operatives
and organizing them.


### Mag Strates and Ministers


no desire to repeat it unnecessarily. Within a dozen years,
however, and over the protest of many local nobles who
saw their hereditary privileges being usurped, this informal network of friends, advisors and trusted servants was
forged into the second branch of the Thousand Scales.
Those who showed particular aptitudes for various fields
were made ministers and given power to hire underlings.
Soldiers were assigned to support the right of these
freshly minted authorities, often escorting new ministers
into the field so that they might make a show of their
power. Others, trusted more for their judgment than a
given talent, were made magistrates and empowered by
the Empress with the right to mete out justice in her
name. As the empire’s infrastructure knitted itself back
together, the need for more and more ministers became
apparent, and the skeleton of the Imperial Bureaucracy
was rapidly forged. Taxes were assessed, levied and collected (often at swordpoint in the first few years). Laws
were established, and examples were made of those
Dragon-Blooded who thought they were above imperial
justice. Slowly but surely, life in the Realm came to be
defined by the regulations of the ministries and the
judgments of the Magistracy.

By RY 38, the core of the ministries was in place. A
single coinage was established and accepted in all corners
of the Realm. Laws were promulgated from one end of the
empire to the other and were enforced universally as well,
demonstrating the Empress’ absolute authority. Those
who sought to defy magistrates or ministers were punished,
and the power of these representatives of empire was
reinforced. Slowly, generations were born who knew nothing but the authority of the Empress and her humble
servants and who accepted it as the natural order of things.

of rule were pale shadows of the mighty armies that the
Shogun and his daimyos had once commanded. While
they were still the mightiest military force on the
Blessed Isle, they were weakened, disorganized and held
together by a tenuous mixture of personal loyalty to the

As the reports ‘of this dissatisfaction rolled in,
the Empress listened to the suggestions of her advisors and spies and arrived at the conclusion that

“much of the unrest could be traced, not to her deci-

sions, but to the fact that those decisions seemed to
have been made arbitrarily.

The key, the Empress felt, was to give the restive
nobles the sensation that they indeed had a hand in the
creation of the Realm's policies. One method of doing
this, of course, would be to involve them in the genuine
legislative process, but this notion was dismissed out of
hand. The Realm was the Empress’, and it was her word
that was law. To invite more cooks into the governance
of the Realm would be to spoil the broth, weakening the
Realm and crippling its decision making.

However, it would certainly be easy enough to present
the appearance of involvement and, thus, to co-opt the
most vocal and annoying elements of the theoretically
loyal opposition. All rhat was required was the creation of
a body wherein the nobles of the Realm might debate
policy and spout oratory to their hearts’ content — all of
which the Empress could then ignore, save for the occasional effort at political maintenance.

In accordance with this notion, the Empress created the two chambers of the Deliberative in RY 103,
just after the turning of the new year. Appointing the
loudest of her critics to the Greater Chamber in numbers sufficient to cause a never-ending logjam in debate,
she granted unto them the right to propose laws — after
suitable debate — that she would then consider. Implied was the notion that her consideration was a mere
formality on the way to approval; ignored was the fact
that the formality was the idea she’d consider their
recommendations at all.

As the Empress had foreseen, the vitriol and energy
that had been directed at her now stayed primarily within
the Deliberative’s chambers. The most venomous critics
of the Empress’ reign were silenced, deprived of their

independent states with her allies, tributaries and

north shore of the Inner Sea, had established positive
relationships in the earliest days of the Empress’ reign
and, so, were given generous terms.

The only term that was immutable in any treaty
made with the Realm involved the Anathema. Other
concessions could be asked for and granted, tariffs lowered and taxes negotiated. But in every single instance
of discussion with the Realm, one constant held: Any
Anathema found were to be turned over to the agents of
the Realm immediately. Failure to do so would be
grounds for war.


### Failure Jn the East


While the Empress’ program reaped success everywhere else, in the former River Province, her efforts were
an unmitigated disaster. Thousands upon thousands of
troops were dashed upon the rock of Lookshy or bled
away by guerrilla actions. Each new defeat increased the
Empress’ determination to bring the Seventh Legion and
its allies to heel, for each disappointment suffered by the
legions damaged their reputation as implacable conquerors and gave new hope to those who chafed under the
yoke of imperial rule. All her efforts were in vain, however, and the Empress was forced to settle for close
relations with nearby states.

THe Dynasty GROWS

The Empress’ consort, Nellens, was a great boon to
imperial fecundity. He fathered two children on the Empress, Sesus and Ledaal, and supported them lavishly from
the Imperial Treasury. Both married young and frequently
and produced a great many children of their own. Possessed of a vicious rivalry, the two competed in all things
and passed their hatreds and desire for the Scarlet Throne
on to their respective broods. Thus began the great game
of the Dynasty, from the maneuverings of the children of
Ledaal and Sesus against one another.

The term “the Dynasty” also dates from the early days ©.

outlived most of her children, both the Realm at large

With the timetable for the announcement of succession set, however, the houses turned on each other in
their great game with renewed vigor. While none dared
to openly eliminate a rival, centuries-long plans were
enacted to sap other houses’ strength so that, by the time
the Empress’ jubilee arrived, they would no longer be
viable candidates. Such was the status quo in the Realm
for almost two centuries, until the playing field was
suddenly upset. by the one-thing no one could have
anticipated: the disappearance of the Empress.


### Peasant Revolts


Peasant rebellions were a long-accepted fact of life
in the Realm, as one generally flared up every time a few
bad harvests ran back-to-back. Most evolve along the
same lines: Dissatisfied peasants band together with the
local Immaculates, find an eloquent leader, band behind her when she voices their grievances and burn a
few local nobles’ houses after dealing with vastly outnumbered militia. At that point, the legions are sent in,
the revolt is put down, and most of the peasants flee
back to their fields. An example is made of the leader
and a few of her followers, and shortly thereafter, all
returns to normal. Trae Leng’s Revolt In RY 299 was
perhaps the most successful of these, as the rebelling
peasants briefly succeeded in breaking through the
gates of Lord’s Crossing and looting the city. Other
notable peasant revolts include the Vengeful Harvest of
RY 326, the Ragara’s Widows’ Uprising of RY 591 and
the Bloodied Scythe Uprising of RY 707. The Ragara’s
Widows’ Uprising was so-called because the levies laid
by the Ragara on their tenant farmers were so ruinous
that it was a matter of popular belief that it was better
financially for farmers to starve to death than to work
their land for their hated overlords.

But the revolt that reared its head in RY 465 was
something altogether different — and more disturbing.

“Called the Unbroken Rushes Rebellion, it appeared
simultaneously in nine prefectures just before the harvest. veywneres peasants laid down their tools and

repeat itself. The Im
Rivers, but it did n

<!--PAGE 29-->
afflict the cities. Rumors swept the Imperial City every
day: The Empress had abdicated, the Empress would summon the Elemental Dragons to put down the rebels, the
Empress was, in fact, the power behind the rebels (all part
of an elaborate plan to see which of her supporters and
children would desert her), and so forth.

Ultimarely, it was not the favor of the Dragons
that saved the Realm, but the deep pockets of the
Imperial Treasury, as augmented by the coffers of
House Ragara. Tons upon tons of foodstuffs were imported to the Blessed Isle from the Threshold and
lands beyond. Agents of the All-Seeing Eye discredited the mysterious Seven Rivers as an Anathema
seeking to lead the righteous subjects of the Empress
astray and lavishly bribed traveling performers and
tale-tellers to make sure their story was spread. And
created by the Empress’ First Age tools, visions of the
Dragons themselves appeared in the sky over the
Imperial City one night, causing all who saw them to
fall to their knees and ask forgiveness. By the end of RY
466, the Unbroken Rushes movement was scattered,
and the peasants had mostly gone back to their labors.

The identity of the mysterious Seven Rivers was
never established, however, nor was he ever caught. In
outlying prefectures he is still regarded as something of
a folk hero, and sightings are reported every few years.

Each is duly investigated by the All-Seeing Eye (as well
as by itinerant monks’ of the Immaculate Order, as
Chejop Kejak takes a very personal interest in this
matter}, and each time, the report comes to nothing.
The legend, however, persists.


### The Modern Day


Five years ago, the Empress disappeared so completely
that it was if she had never been. This occurrence, in and
of itself, was not necessarily unusual. She had taken brief
sabbaticals from the Scarlet Throne before, some lasting as
long as six months. Even in her absence in those times, the
Realm continued to function smoothly, carrying itself
forward on sheer inertia as the mechanisms of government
lurched along with business as usual. None of the Great
Houses made a play for the Throne, wary that this could be
some trick of the Empress’ to see which of her offspring
were too ambitious for their own good. And always, she
returned as if she had never been away, with no word as to
where she had been or what she had done.


### Absence from the Scarlet Throne


Popular romantic songs of the Realm speak of what
the Empress did during her disappearances. They claim
that, during these times, the Empress took it upon herself
to disguise herself as a humble peasant woman and to walk

<!--PAGE 30-->
to and fro in her lands, and up and down in them, so that
she might better understand the suffering of her people.
When she had seen enough, the songs went, she would
return to the Imperial Manse and use what she had learned
to govern the Realm more wisely.

This is, of course, complete hogwash, though politically advantageous hogwash. During her sabbaticals
from the Scarlet Throne, the Empress was most emphatically not laboring sympathetically among the
proletariat of the Realm. Long years of rule and harsh
decisions costing tens of thousands of lives had long
since flensed any care for such things from the Empress.
The Realm and her continued dominance of it had
become all to her, the ends and the means so inextricably intertwined as to be inseparable.

In truth, the Empress’ absence from public life was
spent deep in the depths of the Imperial Manse itself.
There, she labored to propitiate the engines and the things
dwelling therein that had brought her to power, offering
them sacrifices of treasure, blood and souls in exchange for
continued mastery of the devices and continued instruction in their use. Thousands of slaves have been sacrificed
thus over the years, as well as some of the Realm’s most
powerful Dragon-Blooded, a handful of unwary spirits, at
least one imperial consort and the Empress’ youngest
daughter, whose curiosity led her to follow her mother a
trifle too far into the palace.

‘THe ABSENCE LENGTHENS

While everyone pretended it was business as usual
when the Empress first vanished in RY 763, by late 764
those stalking the corridors of power were well aware
that something was seriously amiss. While the Empress
had vanished before, she had never vanished for so
long, and the complete absence of any communication
had senior members of the Thousand Scales, the Dynasty and the Immaculate Order extremely worried.
Several, most notably the venerable Shenesh of House
Iselsi, the ancient and well-respected Minister of the
Imperial Gardens (whose purview also included the
Empress’ Manse), and no fewer than six abbots of the
Immaculate Order convened a meeting at which the
sticky topic of what to do if the Empress were really
gone was.addressed.

The first order of business was to establish rhat no

fact, know what had happened to the
-when or even whether shee

Tae eine ah

would choose a regent to guide the Realm in her place,
doing nothing save occasionally waving to the populace, so that the peasants might avoid growing restive.
Should the Empress not return within seven years, then
steps toward a more permanent succession might be
taken, but in the interim, all were to regard themselves
as caretakers of the Realm against the day when the
Empress returned.

Well satisfied with themselves, the various representatives to the Council of the Empty Throne departed and
immediately began strengthening their positions — subtly
— against the day when competition for the Scarlet
Throne would be thrown open.

The Deliberative did its part to perfection, selecting a feeble mediocrity of House Tepet to keep the
Empress’ seat cushions warm in her absence. That selection, however, was the only aspect of the council's
decision to go as planned. Even before the council, the
allied city-state of Thorns was conquered and transformed into a grim shadowland. Barbarian attacks on
legion outposts increased, and as the various branches
of the Imperial Service strove against each other, the
legions found themselves increasingly ill supported.
Rainy winters and dry summers wrecked crops, and the
taxes levied on tributary states spiraled upward as the
machinery of empire lurched toward a halt. Slowly but
surely, the least loyal tributaries in the Threshold edged
toward revolt and independence, while on the Blessed
Isle, the families of the Dynasty circled each other
warily, waiting for an opening either to ascend or to
bring a rival down.

Meanwhile, the Scarlet Throne sat empty, for not
even Regent Fokuf dared seat himself upon it more than
once. In the streets, they sing songs of the Empress’
triumphant return; in the temples they discuss solemnly
whether the Elemental Dragons are displeased. And in the
Realm’s halls of power, the possibility of civil war becomes
ever more real with every day.

‘THE REALM

The Realm is more than history. Though it is ancient
and seemingly unchanging;.the Realm is still a living,
breathing place. Recently, it.has been a living, breathi

<!--PAGE 31-->
Old Realm stand side by side with elaborately carved
wooden manors, and the streets are sometime
claustrophobically narrow. They are packed dirt or paved
with cobblestones. Some of them are filthy from the
wastes of beasts of burden. In other places children, old
men and the indigent are hired to clean the streets in
return for a loaf of bread or a skin of beer. Many people
walk through cities — the streets are too narrow for carts
and caravans. [n most places, cargo is loaded onto porters
at the city gate and run to its destination.

Through this world, the Dragon-Blooded move like
giants among men. The bright colors of their clothing

stand out against the drab colors of the poor. The aura of :

power. around them makes their subjects. move.aside
without a second thought. Even the bustling glory of the
alriiséems to dim for a moment when one of the
"érrestrial Exalted passes.


### Clothing


Clothing for Dynastic men and women are similarly functional. Both genders may wear long pants,
long skirts or short skirts over pants to social events. It’s
usually considered crass to show one’s legs or torso in
public (Exalted-only parties don’t count as “public”),
but beyond that, there are few rules. In the warmer
climes, Dragon-Blooded who attend trendy parties make

‘a game out of commissioning clothing that creatively

and artfully displays as much skin as possible while still
being considered clothing.

members who have achieved significant power of another
sort or unExalted spouses of good breeding may be honored
with a place at the table with Exalted family members.
UnExalted spouses go through a period of rime — at least
six months — sitting at the lesser tables before they're
accepted at the head table. If the mortal is particularly
famous and well respected, the period can be shorter than
six months without disgracing the Exalted hosts. It is
improper for the hosts to relegate a socially acceptable
spouse or famous and respected family member to the
lower tables for more than five years.

Patricians and the lower classes typically eat large,
household-wide meals, with a large proportion of grains
and vegetables. Those patricians with enough money
do what they can to emulate the Dragon-Blooded,
serving meat and flavoring dishes with spices and herbs.
Less wealthy households with high political ambitions
eat poorly as a family so thar they can serve exotic foods
to important guests.


### Animals


Animals are kept for many purposes in the Realm:
They serve as food, pets, hunting aids, companions and
guards. The unExalted keep food animals and beasts of
burden, but many Terrestrial Exalted keep animals — the
more exotic the better — as pets.

In the hot climes to the south of the Blessed Isle,
Dragon-Bloaded keep austrech as hunters. These animals
are naturally vicious and are kept that way in captivity by
handlers who starve them before hunts. Austrech handler
is a very difficult and dangerous job. In some areas, bears,
boars and hybrocs are hunted for food. Dragon-Blooded
breed or capture these animals for guests to hunt.

Raiton are dirty animals, carrion eaters, and Dragon-
Blooded will only eat them ifno other options are available.
River dragon and siaka ate delicacies, and their skin is also
used in the making of boots and armor. Squid is also
considered a delicacy and reserved for the largest parties.
Mammoth meat is popular for feasts, as so much can be
taken from a single carcass. The Dragon-Blooded prefer
red meat to fish and pork; the less expensive fish is referred
toas patricians’ meat, and the poor eat pork and. what local
river fish they can affor

The lower classes.f

and protective — and can be jealous of that Exalt’s bonds
with other Dragon-Blooded. Strix are believed to be omens
of ill forcune; members of House Ledaal have recently
made use of the birds’ reputation to improve their own
frightening stature among the peasantry.

Dogs are also kept as guard animals; the great cats are
considered too volatile for such duty. A few households
keep giant wolf spiders as guard animals.

"TEMPLES AND SHRINES

An Immaculate temple can be found in every city on
the Blessed Isle; particularly large cities have up to 5,000
temples, mostly to the Dragons but also to the local gods,
so the Immaculates can offer them their due prayers. In
addition, about one-half of small towns have at least one
shrine, and occasionally, a temple is built in a convenient
location close to several small towns. All Dragon-Blooded
households keep personal shrines unless the household is
particularly small, and they are usually tended bya member
of the House who has joined the Immaculate Order.

Smaller shrines can be devoted to a single Immaculate Dragon. In Dragon-Blooded homes, this shrine is
always dedicated to the Dragon of the element that the
house identifies itself with. House members who identify
with a different Dragon sometimes keep personal shrines
in their own rooms.

City temples, particularly those near large populations of Dragon-Blooded, are opulent and beautifully
decorated. Smaller shrines are usually simpler; some are
crude structures of rough wood, while others are elegantly
carved. Those Exalted who particularly value the potential of the faith to keep the common people content
sometimes pay to have beautiful shrines installed in the
towns under their care. Typically, only shrines and temples
actively tended by monks contain valuable goods that
could be stolen. Shrines that aren't constantly tended
either lack adornments altogether or contain decorations that are not easily removed, usually heavy statues
made from common stone.


### Slavery


Contrary to the popular misconception, of the Realm
nation of slaves and slave-owners, the unExalted are.
ibited from owning slaves.

Not all Dragon-Blooded consider slavery to be a
morally sound condition, but they recognize that their
economy depends upon it. Those Exalted who don’t
like slavery either don’t keep personal slaves or buy as
many slaves as possible and treat them as well as
normal servants. Some few are secretly involved in
movements to smuggle slaves out of the Realm, but
even these usually own a small handful of slaves to
keep up appearances.

Most Dragon-Bloaded see the barbarians who are
typically sold into slavery as worthless and ignorant,
unworthy of freedom and incapable of making use of it.
Slavery keeps the cost of goods down, which is a benefit
not to be underrated in these unsettled days. Some truly
believe ‘that their slaves have a better life than they
would have had otherwise — and often, they’re right.

hire from among the local peasantry and pay wages in
food, clothing and staple goods.

It is possible for a Dragon-Blooded to free a slave but
only with the permission of the Deliberative. Each Dragon-
Blood is allowed to free no more than three slaves per
decade. Some Dragon-Blooded use freedom as a reward
for slaves who perform heroically in the service of their
masters. Others use the lure of eventual freedom to prod
new slaves into ever-more dangerous or degrading acts. A
few Dragon-Blooded simply free as many slaves as they
can get away with. It is rare for the Deliberative to deny
a request to free a slave.

The main force of the guardians has little authority
over Dragon-Blooded. At most, they may detain a Dragon-
Blooded who wantonly kills a patrician, citizen or peasant
or deliberately destroys large amounts of public property.
In certain cities (particularly those controlled by Nellens
or Cynis appointees), this may include the deaths of a
great number of slaves, but for the most part, the lives of

someone else's slaves need only be paid for with jade.
Dragon-Blooded may not be detained for killing their
own slaves or the disenfranchised. When a Dragon-
Blood has been detained, a special group of the Guardians
of the Realm is called in — the Imperial Force (non-
Dragon-Blooded quietly refer to them as Dragon
Handlers). This group has the authority to bring a Dragon-
Blooded before the imperial courts. The Greater House
of the Deliberative must approve appointments of officers to the Imperial Force, so it is rare for anyone to end up
here whoisn’tsympathetic to the Dragon-Blooded. There
are a number of Exalted on this force — usually veterans
or Immaculate martial artists — since humans don’t
stand much of a chance if a criminal Dragon-Blood
decides to put up a fight.

Patricians detained by the guardians are brought
before the judges of the imperial courts so long as there
is a reasonable case to be made against them; the

_ guardians have a fair amount of laticude in deciding who
to prosecute. The guardians may hold someone for up to
a week without prosecuting them, although if a prisoner
is particularly influential this can cause problems, and
the guardians take this into account. Some guardians
are also quite amenable to bribery. As long as they don’t
release anyone too dangerous, their superiors typically
turn a blind eye.

The guardians have the authority to settle charges
against peasants and the disenfranchised as they see fit,
without the intervention of a court. For this reason,
most peasants either try to avoid the guardians as much
as possible or to make friends with them, often by paying
“customary gratuities.”

In cities and large towns, the guardians are professional and well organized. In small towns, either one or two
guardians are posted froma larger town or one ortwo locals

Both subsisted on the Empress’ authority, and both have

begun to lose cohesion and influence in the years since
her disappearance.


### Dominions and Prefectures


The Realm is divided into several hundred prefectures, two. of which are large enough to be called
dominions and scores of which are barely worth the
notice of an Exalted Dynast. Each prefecture includes
about 10 to 12 farming towns and several small or large
cities. A prefect oversees the entire prefecture, with a
governor overseeing each town or city underneath the
prefect. Governors collect taxes from the towns and
cities; prefects collect tribute from the governors. Isolated areas with no governor are visited by roving tax
collectors. These rural tax collectors have had a difficult time since the Empress disappeared, and many have
vanished without trace. What follows is an overview of
some of the Realm’s more important prefectures.

ArR]UF DOMINION

Arjuf Dominion borders the Great Inland Sea on the
southwestern shore of the Blessed Isle, next to the Caracal
River. The city of Arjuf itself is the largest port in the
Realm and the home of the merchant navy. It’s also firmly
under the thumb of House Ledaal, as is the dominion.

The prefectship of Arjuf is a powerful position, currently occupied by Ledaal Maris. Maris is mortal, the
daughter of Ledaal Kemar, a powerful Dragon-Blooded
sorcerer. She is cunning, totally loyal to her Exalted father,
about 20 years older than she looks and completely paranoid. Rumor has it that her father uses his powers to slow
her aging so that she’ll be able to run Arjuf fora long time
to come. The two are also lovers and believe very few
people outside the family suspect this. In this, they are
wrong; their relationship is an open secret among the
Exalted nobility of Arjuf.

Ships leave Arjuf every day, journeying to all corners
of the Realm and beyond. The governor of the city,
Rainbow Faith, has made an agreement with the mer-

“chants who. ship goods through the city. If they sell a

certain percentage of their goods within the walls of the
i be given the job. Locals are typically taken toa city for:

answer to the Honorable and Humble Caretakers
of the Common Folk.’ UnExalted Dynasts and

beautiful old buildings made of the local marble still
stand; all the others were torn down so that the material
could be sold. They've been replaced with cheap, tawdry wooden affairs.

Ledaal still pays a few mercenaries to guard the main
thoroughfare through town, as Nishimo sits directly between Arjuf and the third ciry in the dominion, Tuchara.
The usual services catering to travelers still crowd in the
main road — inns, taverns, supply shops of all sorts, tack
shops, blacksmiths and brothels. The inns and taverns on
the main road cater to the Dragon-Blooded and their
tetinues and, as such, retain most of the grandeur of the
city’s golden days. A block or two off the main road,
however, establishments suited to patrician and peasant
travelers can be found, as can more dangerous places,
which the wise travelers eschew. Ledaal’s mercenaries
occasionally earn enough to have’ business elsewhere in
town —— but only occasionally. Mercenaries valuable
enough to regularly earn such a wage rarely remain in such
a pestilent posting as Nishimo for long.

Tuchara started as an artisans’ colony and slowly
grew to become the city it is today, and many artists and
artisans can still be found within its walls. Dragon-
Blooded come from far and wide to have their armor
designed by one of Tuchara’s master armorers or to have
a special outfit made for an imperial party by one of
Tuchara’s dressmakers. Tuchara even has a small school
for Manse architects. Because there is such demand for
these artisans’ work and because people will travel here
from all over the Realm, almost everything in Tuchara is
stunningly beautiful and painfully overpriced.

The inns are built tall and strong, showpieces designed by architecture students. An inn-keeper may go
on at length about which now-famous architect designed
her inn and why he made the architectural choices he
made, usually right before she quotes the outrageous
toom prices. Some of the best restaurants in the Realm
can be found in Tuchara, and several Dragon-Blooded
families own houses in the city or nearby. It has become
a fashionable vacation spot.

<!--PAGE 39-->

### Ayreon Prefecture


Ayreon Prefecture is found in the northwest of the
Blessed Isle and touches briefly on the coastline near
the town of Maya.

Ayreon Prefecture has only one sizeable city, called
Eye of Creation. For a city with such a pretentious name,
it’s politically unimportant. Some minor weaponsmiths
have set up shop here in the last 20 years and have created
an organization they call rhe Talon League. This guild sets
prices and regulates who can make and sell weapons and
who cannot. Few of these artisans show any real talent;
those.who do are usually blocked from joining the League
out of jealousy and greed. The League has a deal with the

2. governor, Starfall Dust. She has the local guardians shut

downanyone who tries to operate without the permission
of-the League, and it gives Dust and the guardians asteep
discount on weapons. The Talon Leagu

no interest in coming into conflict with the:

weapons paid as bribes by the League and a growing
amount of blackmail material to use against local politicians. She has also arranged to have the local branch of the
guardians infiltrated and controlled by her people.

There are two towns in Ayreon: Werck and Maya.
Werck is a mining town and ships sulfur and gemstones to
Tuchara in Arjuf Dominion. The yield is not large, and the
town also relies on local agriculture to stay afloat. The
governor, Joy Canyon, is a smart and even-tempered man.
He only gives mining permits to a few people at a time so
that the veins of gemstone will last the city for some time
yet. In the meantime, he encourages local farming endeavors and slips some of the gemstones to a local household of
House Peleps-in return for a military guard on both the
mines:and the shipments of gems.

outside of rown. The townspeople never talk about the
valley unless pressed, so very few people know of it. If
pushed, they'll admit that it’s a little spooky, but they stay
away from it, and no one ever gets hurt.

In that valley is an entrance to an extensive system of
natural underground caves. One particularly large cavern
contains an altar erected by Stone and his accomplices. On
this altar, Stone sacrifices small animals and jade beads to
a great golden lion spirit that stands four feet tall at the
shoulder. In return, that spirit uses his Charms to keep the
weather favorable. He also uses them to put intruders to
sleep and then to call on Stone (through headacheinducing visions) to remove them.

In contrast, the nearby farming community of Ashra
fervently follows the teachings of the Order of Immaculate
Dragons. As a result, its residents are very friendly to any
Dragon-Blooded who travel through the area, and they
invite all visitors to worship at their well-kept shrine. The
few works of art and jewelry that the farmers have accumulated have gone to adorn this shrine, and it is guarded day
and night by young women from the village.


### Chanos Prefecture


The prefect of Chanos is an actual Dragon-Blooded,
Ragara Nova. He hada reputation as a wastrel and a fop.
He was appointed to the prefecture five years ago both
as a slap in the face and as a means to get him to shape
up — Chanos was not seen as a plum assignment in
those days, and a prefecture generally carries a lot of
responsibility with it.

The plan has worked almost too well. The city of
Chanos itself, where Nova has based himself, is a port
city and a military town. It lies on the northeastern
border of the Blessed Isle, not far from the frozen North,
and many troops and military ships move through the
port. It is a rugged place with comparatively few creature comforts, and some of the troops there were not
afraid to show what they thought of this fop. Nova
quickly learned he would have to shape up if he wanted
the respect that he was used to getting by default in
other places. In so doing, he has displayed something
that no one expected —a keen grasp of military strategy
and the: attending politics. He has also developed a
friends hip with Tepet Ejava, the Roseblack.

The family has done its best to keep up Nova’s
utside of House Ragara asa useless child

Other than services for the military, towns in Chanos
Prefecture produce mostly mined goods such as marble,
gemstones and some substances used by sorcerers. The
towns also mine iron ore and work it into usable shape,
resulting in heavy, choking air and hopelessly polluted
water supplies. The entire area is more gray than green.
Local towns have developed a pattern of situating the
mines deep in the hills, putting the town itself off to the
side, and building any processing equipment downstream
from there. Between illness and overwork, lifespans in
these towns are often short.

Many artisans have set up shop in the area; the
materials are cheaper if they don’t have to be shipped.
Artisans who have the money to settle elsewhere usually
do so, and as a result local artisans are rarely the cream of
the crop. Their prices are low.

Nova has been more generous than his predecessor
about stationing small military units in local mining towns
to cut down on robbery. Because of this, the town governors are better than many about paying their taxes.


### Eagle Prefecture


Eagle Prefecture is the western-most prefecture on the
Blessed Isle and the gate to the western isles. The prefect,
Orchid Coral, is known to be in the pocket of House Sesus
and even married one of their unExalted sons, Sesus Lilon.
This is much of the reason why the major port city of Eagle,
Eagle’s Launch, is not as well used as Arjuf. Coral doesn’t
keep secret the fact that she uses her position to badger
anyone visiting the properties of the Peleps family in the
northern isles. She also isn’t nearly as evenhanded in her
policies as Rainbow Faith, so many traders have found it
cheaper to sail the extra distance to and from Arjuf.

The governor of Eagle’s Launch, Orchid Sand, is
Coral’s son. He has no love for his father and isn’t as loyal
to Sesus as his mother is, but he likes the money they pay.
Occasionally, he’ll take money under the table from someone who wants to get around Coral’s rules.

Eagle’s Launch serves mainly as a distribution center
for products and resources from the isles, such as fish,
shellfish, siaka, squid, whales, carved coral and seashells
and seaweed. A few siaka‘and whale hunters base themselves out of Eagle’s. Latinch ailing. out for days or even
weeks at a time on strong ressel a

towns. Even those th

<!--PAGE 41-->
usually do their best to deal civilly with these spirits and go
about their business. Sometimes, such closeness inspires
religious zealotry, however — on both sides of the issue.

The small community of Glalo maintains a quiet
practice out on its boats of sacrificing three fish from the
first catch of each day to the local weather spirits. This first
small indiscretion eventually lead to others. The town is
very quiet about its “superstitions” and would laugh them
off as silly old traditions if anyone asked, but the truth is
that the townspeople have slowly and steadily fallen into
heresy. A Ragara who passed through the area not long ago
picked up just enough to guess and, eager to cause problems
for the Sesus, sent word to the Immaculate Order that it
should send a monk to check things out. In these hectic
and trying times, a few suspicions about a small town are a
low priority, but certainly, someone will eventually come
to look in on things. Glalo is an isolated community that
dislikes travelers and visitors. There are no inns and only
one tavern, which does its best to make non-locals feel
uncomfortable and unwelcome.

In the community of Guin, on the other side of the
prefecture, the opposite is true. Its people have embraced
the Order of the Immaculate Dragons to what some might
say isan unhealthy degree. An old couple was burned alive
after they were caught setting out milk for a small catspirit. The event shook everyone up, and the town has
desperately kept the affair a secret. Guin also has a problem
with a river dragon that has been eating the local wildlife.
The townspeople have had to go farther and farther from
shore in order to fish, and they've lost a couple of boats to
storms that way. They fear to send for help, in case those
sent discovered the town’s secret.

The mid-sized town of Cloaka, governed by Prism
Flame; is best known for its horse-breeders. The local
breed, called firemanes, are bred for speed and endurance.
They aren’t particularly strong and are rarely used for
pulling carriages or hauling packs, but they're perfect for
long-distance races and for traveling. Several Dynastic
families visit Cloaka to purchase horses. Sometimes they'll
bring a favorite mare for the purpose of breeding her with

the local stock. firemanes are tall, long-lined horses with.
beautifully arched necks. They're black or dark red, and
cir long; flowing manes and tails are always'a deep red. “


### Incas Prefecture


Incas Prefecture, located in a wides

grains and vegetables are major exports

Incas has two cities: Sion and.

produces is common, everyday material for patricians and
peasants, but it does put out asmall amount of high-quality
material for the Dynasts.’The captain of Sion’s guardians, Oak, is the son of a
patrician mother and a Sidereal father. Most people believe that his father is dead and was human, but Oak keeps
in touch with his father, a member of the outlawed Cult of
the Illuminated, and knows exactly what he is. Oak has
occasionally smuggled a newly Exalted Solar off of the
Blessed Isle and to his father. He also keeps a quiet eye on
the actions of the Dragon-Blooded and the Immaculate
Order and sends word on to his father about anything that
sounds interesting. While he believes in his father’s work,
he’s still more concerned with maintaining his own safety
than with doing everything he can to help.

A few artisans reside in Sion and design particularly
beautiful clothing, but most of the material produced here
is made in bulk. Once a year, in the month of Resplendent
Fire, a festival is held in Sion. Clothing designers come
from all over the Blessed Isle to display their designs. Sion
provides free room and board for these designers, and the
festival, in turn, brings ina great deal of business. This time
is when many year-long contracts are signed to ship Sion
cloth all over the Blessed Isle.

Noble is half-military town and half-marketplace. It
is located on the furthest southwest point of the prefecture, where the South stretches its arms close to the
Blessed Isle. It was originally built to help repel any
invaders from the South and to serve as a staging ground
when sending troops into the region. Since this resulted
in so many people moving through the town, it also grew
into a marketplace for the various foodstuffs and animals
grown and raised within the prefecture, as well as for a
number of other products.

Now that forays into the Threshold are fewer and
farther between and several legions have been disbanded,
Noble is shrinking. There is less traffic, sales are down,
prices have been lowered, and local farmers have had to
tighten their belts. Entire streets stand empty now.

The Palace Sublime stands in Incas Prefecture, just

outside of Sion. House Iselsi has a strong presence here,

### Juche Prefecture


The quarries at the foot of the Imperial Mountain
(just outside of the city of Juche) are the source of most
of the stone used for building and most of the jade used for
coins. The city itself houses laborers for the mines,
artisans who carve the stone and design buildings and a
small colony of Mountain Folk. Juche Prefecture really
should be a dominion, as it includes all of the land from
Juche city to the Imperial City. Only the Imperial City
and the land from there to the sea are not included (see
“The Scarlet Prefecture”).

The governor of Juche, Gemstone Jade, is friendly
with the Mountain Folk and consults with their princes
often. She is careful to be seen as taking their advice
under consideration, however, rather than being under their control. She has quietly taken one of their
number as a lover.

There are three princes of the local Mountain Folk:

Most of House Nellens lives in the city of Juche while
they negotiate with House Ragara for a wing of the palace.
Nellens Ranes, one of the few Dragon-Blooded members
of the house, is trying to figure out how best to make use of
the family’s proximity to the Mountain Folk in their bid for
the Throne. Unfortunately for him, he’s a bit of a loud,

pushy person, and while not overly obnoxious, he hasn’t
particularly won the admiration or friendship of the elegant, stolid Mountain Folk.

There are four mid-sized towns in the ¢ area, most
with a reasonable contingent of Dynasts in them. Plenty
of people want to be close to the Imperial City, but they
also want some distance now and then. As a result,
many Dragon-Blooded keep “get-away” homes in the
towns of Juche Prefecture.

House Iselsi is perhaps the only house that has no
family members with publicly known homes in the area. It
does, however, maintain two small pieces of property
through very distant human relations. One of these is a
small inn located in a town called Spider’s Crossing. The
inn is an unassuming and popular place to stop for travelers, and it has several rooms hidden underground for family
members who need a place to hide or hold meetings. The
employees are all distant family, and all keep their eyes and
ears open — although not all of them know who it is
they're spying for. The second piece of property is a small
farm not far outside of Juche. Certain agents of the All-
Seeing Eye use the farm asa resting place and message post.

Enough farming is carried on in the area to support the
needs of the prefecture. Very little food is exported, however, and many non-staple foods are imported.

A particularly large population of slaves can be
found in Juche Prefecture — there’s a fair amount of
heavy labor to be done in the area, and there are many
Dragon-Blooded living locally. The city of Juche has an
extensive ghetto, and the prefecture’s towns havea larger
proportion of poor people than most. There may be
plenty of stone for buildings coming out of the mines, but

_. The swift

With the recent loss of Tepet’s legions, the morale of
Lord’s Crossing and the nearby towns has dropped precipitously. Many husbands, wives and children mourn
family-members lost in the North. Tepet has done its best
to avoid having these emotions focused on itself. The
family spends what money it can afford on solemn parades
and memorials honoring the dead, listing their own lost
children right next to the names of the soldiers who died
under their command. In this way, they subtly remind
everyone that they lost family as well, while simultaneously assuring everyone that they see other families’
losses as being just as important as their own, and that they
must rebuild together.

The entire area has the feeling of a sick patient that
has stumbled and now wavers trying to get back onto its
feet. Sawyer Shadow, the governor of Lord's Crossing
City, and Carpenter Reason, the prefect, are working
crogether to bring as much traffic, trade and money to the
area as possible — and to find new recruits for the Tepet
forces. The two officials are doing their best to balance
the people’s need for a time of mourning, the economic
necessity for visitors, contracts and shipping and the
desire of House Tepet to rebuild its shattered forces.
Shadow is the dark-skinned, red-haired granddaughter of
a Dynast anda Southern prince; she has her grandfather's
Southern fire and her grandmother’s keen economic
sense. Reason is a self-assured patrician born of a long
line of powerful patricians, and he learned the labyrinthine mysteries of politics at an early age.

The two politicians have planned several large festivals over the next few years. Publicly, they’re saying it’s
to help the people move on and to celebrate the brave
dead. Privately, they also want the influx of people and
recruits such festivals will bring.

The dominion exports a particularly large range of
goods and services; this is why Lord's Crossing, an inland
city, has become such acenter of trade. Mining for ores and
stone is carried out in the hills. There are two local
processing centers in Lord’s Crossing for ores; the rest is
shipped raw. Marble, jade and other gemstones can all be

“found in modest amounts in the hills. Farmland is particu-

larly fertile along the river, and local farmers produce rice,
