LIFE CYcLe

Immaculate monks are a vital part of the communities
of the Realm. Through the ceremonies the monks perform, they mark the life cycles of the Realm's inhabitants
and show their place in the scheme of things. No baby is
born in the Realm without an Immaculate pronouncing
his name for the first time; until a priest does so, the child
is simply “girl” or “boy.” While the Dynasts may have
house priests of their own blood, peasants or owners of
remote estates may wait months or even years until the
opportunity arises to formally name their children.

Likewise, Immaculates oversee rites of death and
marriage and those of coming of age. It is an Immaculate who lays the first torch on the cadaver’s funeral
pyre and who informs the community that he who was
once a boy is now a man.

"TEMPLES AND MONASTERIES

Apart from wandering monks, Immaculates generally labor in one of two sorts of buildings: temples and
monasteries. Temples are dedicated to the function of
the Immaculates within the community. The temple is
where services are held and classes taught and where
appropriate offerings are made in the sight of the congregation. Except at rare times, temples are open to the
public, so that the common folk may contemplate, pray
and gaze on enlightenment whenever they wish. Monks
who are assigned to temples are outgoing rather than
contemplative, and many in rural areas or the Threshold
also walk routes among the people.

Monasteries, on the other hand, are devoted entirely
to contemplation and study. Often, they are staffed and
occupied by the devotees of a single Elemental Dragon,
and the form and decoration of the monastery is dictated
by this fact. Monks share cells in monasteries, usually two
or four to a room, and spend their days in contemplation,
prayer, gardening and whatever specialized tasks (such as
transcription, illumination of manuscripts or musical
composition) the monastery specializes in. Monasteries
are not open to any outside the Immaculate Order, and
trespassers are gently:but firmly shown the gate. Samesex monasteries, which; are rare, are referred to as abbeys,
and any monk who heads either sort of monastic community is referred to.as an abbo

Enlightened Masters). For holy days and special occasions, monks also have ceremonial robes, which are
intricately decorated versions of their workaday clothes.

Monks who wear anything other than their prescribed robes, if caught, are immediately clad in the gray
robe of the postulant for a month, as a reminder to be
humble and to adhere to their appropriate station. Those
who manage to duck this punishment find themselves
the victims of bad luck, which grows steadily worse until
they mend their ways.


### Celibacy


Immaculate monks are celibate from the moment
they take their first vows and are expected to hold to
this standard throughout their lives. Masturbation is
permitted in order to allow the monks a stable psyche,
but sexual contact with others is prohibited, regardless
of the genders involved. The prohibition is not so much
moral as professional. A monk who is devoting her
energies to the pleasures of the flesh is not devoting
them to matters of the spirit. A divided focus does not
allow for proper contemplation, hinders meditation
and prevents the monk from performing her duties in
the most efficient fashion.

Monks caught dabbling in carnal matters are
stripped of their rank and robes immediately and expelled from the Order. This is done more in sorrow than
in anger, but there are no exceptions to the rule. On the
rare occasion that a monk becomes pregnant, she is
allowed to stay within the cloister until such time as she
gives birth. Then, after sufficient time for recovery, she
is expelled. The baby, however, remains with the Order
and will be raised to someday enter its ranks. Some of
the most notable monks in the Immaculates’ history
have come from such humble origins, the cause of a
great deal of theological debate and reflection among
the Immaculate Order's philosophers.

Diet ,

As a nionk ascends the Coils of the Immaculate
Order, his diet is more and more steadily restricted. The
reasoning behind this is simple: The purer the diet, the
purer the body, and the purer the body, the purer the
spirit. Monks who break their dietary strictures are
subject to purification rituals that take five days. During
that time, they are allowed no food and only water to
drink, and they must spend their days in meditation,
prayer and reflection on precisely why they chose to
violate the dietary laws. Repeat offenders are often
demoted ranks until they can learn proper discipline.
For if they cannot discipline their palates, what chance
do they have of disciplining their spirits?

Monks who attempt to hew to the dietary restrictions of those more advanced in their studies are generally
regarded as overly ambitious. Piety is all well and good,
but appropriate piety is better, and attempting to shame
one’s peers by overshadowing them is contrary to the
Order’s best interests. Those who restrict themselves
voluntarily are generally let off with a lecture and a
suggestion that patience is rewarded. Those who are
simply ambitious are inevitably found out and given on
the exercise yard to the monks whose diet they attempted
to emulate for the wrong reasons.

job of this are publicly chastised and shaved in front of
others of their coil.

The shaving of the head is considered a reminder to
each individual monk to beware of vanity. Hair is the
symbol of devotion to the self, and the time required to
groom it, rend icand otherwise care for it is time not spent
in contemplation of the Immaculate Texts. By shaving
his head, the postulant symbolically removes that devotion to the self and accepts his role as one among the
many members of the Order.

It is considered extremely arrogant for a postulant to
have shaved his head before entering the temple. Doing
so indicates that the would-be Immaculate has chosen
the Order for himself, rather than being chosen, and such
cocksure behavior is rarely rewarded.


### Weapons


A large portion of the [mmaculate study involves the
martial arts, both armed and unarmed. Archery, swordplay, spear-work and knife expertise are all raught at the
various abbeys, along with unarmed combat, giving the
Immaculates a well-deserved reputation for being fearsome warriors. There are no restrictions on weapons that
a monk might use —- edged, blunt or otherwise. What is
compulsory is that the monks achieve excellence in its
use. The Immaculate Order is no place for dilettantes.

Poison, on the other hand, is expressly forbidden by
the Immaculate Texts. Poison allows the weak to strike
down the strong and the lowly to slay the mighty, and as
such, it is a contravention of the Order’s stated purpose.
That does not mean, however, that the Order’s herbalists
have not spent extensive hours studying poisons. However, they have done so strictly in order to find cures for
anything from the venom of serpents to the most vile
concoctions imagined by man.

the Bronze faction of Sidereal Exalted, those who orchestrated the slaughter of the Solar and Lunar Exalted for
the greater good. From behind the scenes, these Sidereals
maintain ‘an.active hand in the affairs of the Order,
particularly as regards divining the location of new Anathema and stamping out destabilizing heresies.

### Chejop Kejak


Chief among the Sidereal survivors is Chejop Kejak,
the creator of the Immaculate Philosophy and éminence
grise of the Immaculate Order. While the Mouth of Peace
controls the day-to-day decisions, it is Kejak who steers
the greater course, directing the Order’s ultimate direction and with it, the course of the Realm. If a Mouth of
Peace disagrees with him, Kejak ourmaneuvers her or, if
necessary, outlives herand sees toa more pliable successor's

Immaculate Order. Some have temples or monasteries

Bronze faction’s cause. They, along with Kejak, created
the Immaculate Order and view it as theirs to do with as

the reign of the Celestial Exalted} and each consoles

Like Kejak, the Bronze elders are known and honored
within the Immaculate Order as particularly studious and
powerful monks. They have free run of the temples and
command the respect and obedience of all but the most
powerful Dragon-Blooded Immaculates. Those are permitted, at least publicly, to retain their authority.

‘THE OuTER CIRCLE

As various of the Bronze faction have passed away
over the years, some few have been reborn. The Inner
Circle tracks these births and Exaltations with feverish
accuracy, spending many hours each day ordering and
reading auguries.

The result of these searches is the younger membership of the Bronze faction, Sidereals reborn into the world
after the great betrayal of the Celestials. Younger, less
powerful and more aggressive than the Inner Circle, they
cannot help but regard the tales of the First Age as
somewhat exaggerated and, perhaps, apocryphal. They
sense that Kejak and his cronies will not live forever, and
wait—some less patiently than others — for the day when
the Immaculate Order will be theirs.


### The Bronze Faction and Belief


Having created the Immaculate faith out of whole
cloth, members of the Bronze faction cannot ultimately
believe in it, at least not as written. However, even the old
guard respect its philosophies and practices, drawn as they
are from their own beliefs and approaches. And for many,
it is soothing to lose themselves in the chants and.the
rituals, allowing the wave of belief to wash over. them and
affirm for them the rightness of what they have done. :

Ona more practical level, the members of the Bronze
faction hew to the Order’s strictures bec: they are at

households make gifts to the Order in an attempt to buy
luck from the Elemental Dragons), holdings (huge tracts of
land all across the Realm and the Threshold have been
willed to the Order, either out of piety or as bribes in hopes
of greasing the skids for a postulant’s acceptance), military
power and spiritual authority, the Order wields a great deal
of clout. Moreover, the Mouth of Peace and her advisors —
not to mention the shadowy members of the Bronze
faction — are well aware of this.

The Order generally does not stoop to dabble in
secular affairs directly. To do so too often would compromise its spiritual authority, after all. Instead, the Mouth of
Peace picks and chooses her battles, and when she decides
it is time to intervene, then she does so without reserve.
The pressures the Order can bring to bear are enormous:
sermons read at services every morning, pointed interpretations of the Immaculate Texts that are released for public
perusal and, most of all, the threat of being denounced. A
denunciation from the Mouth of Peace carries the weight
of a hammer blow, as it is popular belief within the Realm
that curses follow her displeasure, and the Elemental
Dragons themselves lend their strength to such efforts.
Whether this is actually the case or the result of the Bronze
faction and the Empress working behind the scenes to
strengthen the illusion is of no matter to hoi polloi. They
simply see even the mightiest Dynasts toppled afrer an
accusation from the Mouth of Peace, and in this act, they
see the power of the Immaculate Order confirmed.


### Active Interference


A careful examination of the chain of being as’
imagined in the Immaculate Texts shows the Order's
place as outside the rest of society, steadying it rather
than partaking of it. If the monks do their job properly,
then respect for place is instilled in all, and society
functions smoothly. If not, however, it is the monks’
responsibility to try to restore proper order.

What that means, in the broad view, is that monks
rarely dabble in politics. It is not their purview, and it is
not their place, and to express their viewpoint in secular
affairs is to overstep their bounds. In cases where a local

-abbotsees something she feels is inappropriate; it is likely

ispatch arunner witha particularly fcring

<!--PAGE 78-->
deal to mobilize the wrath of the Immaculates; wanton
rape and pillage, taxes that drive entire villages into
starvation, gross dereliction of duty on the part of ministers, magistrates and landowners and so forth. After all, by
acting in this fashion to unkink the rightful order of being,
the Immaculates are themselves overstepping their bounds.
It is not something they undertake lightly, and the wrong
they are setting right must be something truly horrific.
Mere embezzlement or cruelty is not enough; the very
spirits of the land must cry out for vengeance and correction before the Immaculates will act.

The first step is an innocuous enough one; the plastering of posters with the monks’ declaration of purpose on
them. Usually, these go up all over the temple and, in many
cases, on whatever walls are handy in the area. Attempts
to stop the postering are met with polite refusal, though
those who seek to physically interfere with the process find
themselves on their backsides in the dust. The monks take
this sort of thing very seriously.

If five days of postering do not have the desired
effect, then the town outside the temple is treated to a
procession of monks. Most hold banners with appropriate passages from the Immaculate Texts drawn on them.
Others play cymbals or drums. None are armed, and none
speak. Instead, the procession wends its way through the
streets in a seemingly random pattern — but one that
always finds its way to the palace, place of business or
estate of the offender whom the monks wish to chastise.
Once they arrive, the monks stand a silent vigil, the only
sound the beating of percussion instruments. Then, ata
prearranged signal, they turn and trace their path back to
the temple, their disapproval clear.

On occasion, these processions are accompanied by
vigils at other places within the city. This depends on how
large the local Immaculate community is, the size of the
city and so forth. If the site of unrest is a major city, then
itis entirely possible for a half-dozen processions of monks
to be snaking their way through the streets at once and for
a dozen more individual acolytes to be performing public
fasts or prayer vigils in busy intersections.

Soldiers, for their part, prefer not to interfere with
monks engaging in this sort-.of behavior. Superstition
dictates that doing so btings the unfortunate soul who
tries a decade of bad luck: Practicality indi¢ates that an
city watchman who.at
monk is going to ge
watchmen usually do
the monks and to

<!--PAGE 79-->
When the decision is made to use force, the monks
march out of the temple and find their target or
targets. Without warning, they attack and, in most
cases, make short work of their opponents. They then
move on to other targets, declaring their righteous
purpose and calling out the conditions under which
they will return to the temple.

Few rebellions are limited to just the Immaculates,
however. As the monks wreak havoc, the peasantry and
merchant classes, used to taking their behavioral cues
from the monks, join the rebellion as well. In a best-case
scenario, they follow the monks in reasonably good
order, swelling the ranks of the crowd and lending force
to the Immaculates’ statement. Much of rhe time, however, the citizenry is inflamed by what they perceive as
the vigilante spirit, and the populace transforms into a
mob. What follows is an orgy of fire, looting and destruction, and even the monks can become caught up in it. If
they perceive the offenders who caused them to take to
the streets as sufficiently wicked, the monks will condone
fire and sword to drive them out.

In some cases, the action of the mob results in the
creation of temporary theocracies, until a sizable legion
detachment arrives to restore order. Until such time as the
rightful government reasserts itself, the monks are the only
law, and the temple hierarchy is the one that organizes firefighting details, rations food and sees to basic human
continuity in the wreckage.

Once their point is made, the monks retreat to the
temple. However, if they feel that arriving troops will
reinstate the conditions that forced them to act in the
first place, the Immaculates will fight. They do not
expect to win under such circumstances; it is, after all,
not their place to topple the entire Realm. They will,
however, fight to the last and to the best of their ability.
This can make for long, drawn-out battles, particularly
if the troops fighting the priests have been brought up
to revere and fear them.

Monks begin their martial arts training the day they
join the Immaculate Order. After being accepted by the
Paragon of Sextes Jylis, they are shown to their quarters,
issued their robes and then herded into one of the Palace
Sublime's many exercise courts. There, Dragon-Blooded
and mortal alike are tested ruthlessly by one of the many
Puissant and Humble Instructors who labor in the Palace
Sublime, monks whose duties consist solely of training
those less skilled than they. Relentlessly poked, prodded,
stretched and forced to defend themselves, the new
acolytes have their skill level and limits quickly established. The Humble Instructors are not gentle, and many
acolytes suffer blackened eyes, bloodied noses or worse.
However, this is all in accordance with the greater plan,
one that molds rough, unpolished seekers into assured,
confident monks.

For the first year, acolytes line up every morning
shortly after sunrise in the exercise yard to which they have
been assigned. There, they are drilled relentlessly in the
basic forms, stances and moves of Five Dragon Style, the
martial arts school upon which all of the other Immaculate
schools are based. A mixture of simple punches, kicks,
throws and blocks, Five Dragon Style is more a primer on
how to move and how to study than ir is an actual combat
style, though a suitably skilled practitioner is more than a
match for an unschooled thug or three.

There are five ranks within Five Dragon Style,
each named after one of the elements. At the turn of
each season, the students are tested on their progress.
Those who demonstrate sufficient skill and discipline
are promoted to the study of the next rank, while those
who fail are punished and forced to repeat their studies
— and the grueling examination — until they pass. An
acolyte remains an acolyte until such time as he has
completed all five examinations to the satisfaction of
his instructor. If one of the Puissant and Humble

- Instructors feels that a student will never achieve
sufficient progress, that student is expelled from the
Order, regardless of how devout his observations or
how keen his mind might be. To be Immaculate, after
all, istostrive to approach perfection on all levels, and

### Further Studies


When, after a year as an acolyte, a newly ordained
monk selects the Elemental Dragon whose teachings he
will follow, he also selects his primary martial arts school.
Each of the schools of the Five Elemental Dragons has a
series of styles associated with its devotees, anda monk will
not be allowed to study other styles until he has made
significant progress in his native one.

Monks who have selected a school are instructed by
monks known as Diligent and Skilled Teachers, who
proceed to drill their new students both morning and night
for as long as they remain atthe Palace Sublime.

There are, unsurprisingly, five levels of mastery to
each of the schools. Examinations are given to students without their foreknowledge, based upon the
opinions of their instructors as to whether or not they
are ready. Examinations test the spirit, stamina and
physical prowess of the candidate. Often, a student is
called upon to sing hymns or recite sutras from the
Immaculate Texts while defending himself or to hold
a single stance for an hour while insults are shouted at
him and blows delivered that stop a whisker’s breadth
from his face.

Monks who pass are honored and advanced to the
next level of instruction, while those who fail are given
neither praise nor scorn. They simply return to the ranks
of their peers, bruised and bloodied, to await the next
opportunity to prove themselves.

The first three years of training focus on unarmed
combat. Weapons training follows thereafter and becomes an increasingly important part of the curriculum.
Each student is allowed to chose his initial style and
may not deviate from that style’s study until he has
passed at least two examinations in it. A monk is not
allowed to study any school other than his own until he
has mastered his base style, and even then, it is frowned
upon for the unExalted.

Depending upon whether a monk is human or
Exalted, he is taught different techniques. Many of the
Dragon-Blooded-only techniques involve incorporating the use of Essence in one’s movements, combining
the physicality of the martial arts with the supernatural
potence of Essence. Ne arts :
taught to the human.

<!--PAGE 81-->
THe Heresy OF THE ANTITHESES

One of the most persistent, yet smallest
heresies against the Immaculate Order is the
Heresy of the Antitheses. Its belief, simply
stated, is that the Immaculate Dragons will be
reinvested in flesh as their Antitheses, though

whether this is to punish the wicked or to herald
the end of the world is still the subject of the

debate. The Immaculate Order as a whole has
no patience for this sort of thing and shows
unusual ruthlessness when stamping out the
Heresy of the Antitheses wherever it appears.


### Heresy


The Immaculate Order has a curious attitude toward heresy. On one hand, it mounts no crusades and no
witch-hunts. Secure in its correctness, it sees no need to
legitimize any would-be rivals by paying active attention to them. The continued existence of the Realm
and the obvious blessings bestowed upon the Immaculate monks are all that is needed to convince others of
the doctrine’s accuracy. Only those heretics who actively advocate tearing down the Immaculate Order are
dealt with publicly and then with a great deal of sadness.
Here, it would seem, is a soul who has not advanced on
the road to Exaltation and perfection and who would be
better off trying again before he can distract others from
their proper path.

However, behind the scenes, the Order can be jealous. Should a particular heresy, whether it be a deviant
reading of the Immaculate Texts or a particularly atavistic resurrection of the Hundred Gods faith, begin to gain
momentum, it is swiftly and efficiently taken apart.
Itinerants infiltrate the heresy and tear it to pieces from
the inside. Failing that, they'll deal with the heretics
more permanently and then vanish, allowing the carnage
to be ascribed to the wrath of the Dragons.

THE : HUNDRED Gops

from growing mighty on the worship of men and enslaving the populace and reduces corruption in the Celestial
Bureaucracy, as the Little Gods are less inclined to favor
one individual or group over another.

Another unstated reason is to separate the peasantry
from communication with spirits, who might contradict
the Immaculate faith and tell stories of the days before
the Order was in place. The vast majority of spirits bear
no love for the Immaculates, but do have respect for
them. In accordance with their faith, Immaculate monks
make generous and proper offerings to the spirits. This,
generally, is enough to engender at least enough goodwill
that spirits will not assault an Immaculate on sight.
However, attempts to proselytize among the spirits —
who, of all beings in the Realm, know better — are
generally met with varying degrees of refusal.

The Dragon-Blooded of the Dynasty lead long and
unusual lives — and not just in terms of their political
maneuverings and legendary adventures. From birth their
lives differ from those of the patricians and peasants

tirement — all are creatures of a different color for
individuals who live for centuries and wield the power of
the Elemental Dragons.

Houses

The Dynastic houses wield an incredible amount of
influence in the Realm. It is the fact that they fight
amongst themselves so much that keeps them from being
an even greater force to be reckoned with than they are.
The Empress deliberately fostered such differences in an
attempt to keep anyone from growing strong enough to
challenge her own position.

V'neef isa dignified, yet unassuming woman. Her hair
is long and bark-brown, worn braided down her back. She
is slender and unusually short. She wears brown or green
breeches and tunics for most occasions, although she has
several simple, silken gowns for social occasions. Her one
indulgence is a set of armor so finely wrought it fits her
almost like a second skin.

Wneef has no less ambition than her brothers and
sisters, but she chooses to exercise it in different ways.
Her schemes are long-lasting and quiet, like her personality. Her children don’t constantly and consciously
work to serve all of her interests the way some of her
siblings’ children do. Yet, she has subtly and carefully
shaped them, paying perhaps more attention to them
than most Dragon-Blooded parents do. As a result, most
of them exert their efforts in directions that she chose for
them, whether they realize it or not.

Economics

V'neef is careful not to claim too much tribute from
her satraps and is reasonable about making investment
deals with satraps she has some trust in. Because of this,
most of her satraps still pay the tribute she claims. The
Empress liked V’neefs potential to upset some of the
existing power structures among the Dynasts, so House
V'neef receives a larger subsidy than its size and age would
seem to indicate. The Empress’ replacement probably
won’t see things the same way, so V’neef has strong
incentive to get involved in the fight for the Throne.

V’neef not only has control of most of the vineyards
across the Realm, but she has also branched out into

the barrels and other wine-making equipment her house

V’neef's control over the merchant navy is arecent
matter. The post was granted to V’neef by the Empress
a decade before her disappearance as a source of revenue
to allow the young Dynast to build up her newly created
house — whoever controls the merchant navy is entitled to a cut of the duties collected by its customs
cutters. Prior to this it, the merchant navy was under
the control of House Peleps, and its removal from the
Peleps’ domain caused them no small hardship, as they
were left to bear the expense of the imperial fleet
without the benefit of customs duties. House V’neef’s
control over the merchant navy is a critical financial
and military asset. While the house is too small to fill all
the important posts with blood relations, individuals
who occupy prominent places in the merchant navy
chain of command are all unquestioningly loyal to the
house, and the V’neefspymasters have thoroughly purged
the organization's ranks of those loyal to House Peleps.

V’neef Satepis is V’neef's youngest unExalted son.
He’s young, born in Realm Year 751, but he has a sharp

Blooded twice his age, Cynis Lelet. Personally, he finds
House Cynis distasteful, bue he understands that it’s

House RAcARA (EARTH)

Ragara himself is retired, ailing and perfectly happy
to be free of politics and manipulation. His second son,
Banoba, carries on quite well in his father’s absence.
Banoba is a tall, stocky man who moves and talks slowly
and thinks quickly. He first rose to prominence by taking
advantage of others’ tendency to think him dull-witted.
Even once others learned better, however, his wits kept
him going. He has married a succession of intelligent and
wealthy patricians in the interests of the house but
maintained only a single lover throughout the years.
Heral, a distant Dragon-Blooded nephew, caught Banoba’s
heart: more than 100 years ago, and the two have been
inseparable since.

: Banoba speaks for the family, and when he speaks
‘publicly, nearly the entire family follows his lead. He leads
the house with a strong hand and is unafraid:to tell his

wings and children what they’re going to ds. vith their

MAjor LINES

~Most of House Ragara lives in the Imperial Palace.
There are two major households, however, that do not
live with the bulk of the family. The first of these is the
Soras household. This household makes its home in the
northern isles and has provided land and buildings for
Ragara and a few of his friends to retire on. The Soras are
primarily a merchant family and do a great deal of
business through the port of Chanos. They have kept up
many of the family’s Guild contacts.

The Calel household lives in Juche Prefecture on a
sprawling rural estate. The Calels breed hunting dogs,
spend much of their time at parties and happily entertain
important politicians and merchants for House Ragara.
The younger members of this household travel exrensively, actively keeping up contacts and alliances with
other houses, reminding debtors of their debts to the house
and otherwise presenting Banoba with an excellent picture of the house's holdings.

Economics

House Ragara has been called “the imperial bank”
behind its back, particularly since the Empress’s disappearance. Ragaras will loan money to anyone and everyone as
long as they think they can get it back with interest. With
their money, they’re happy to hire mercenaries and legbreakers to collect debts owed them by patricians and
subject princes. [t might seem like a loss in the short term,
but it only takes one successful enforcement to encourage
the next 10 people to pay off their debts. They like to come
across as reasonable and helpful, always willing to help
someone find an alternate way to pay off a debt she can’t
handle. Some members of the house delight in loaning
money to those who won’t be able to repay it, simply so that
they can push the debtor into repaying the debt in much
more valuable tender than money. Ragara has a large
number of favors it can call in from all sorts of interesting
sources due to this practice.

On occasion, the Ragaras find that a Dragon-Blooded
has reneged on a contract or otherwise refused to pay back
funds they have loaned. House Ragara has no interest in
encouraging other houses to use violence in matters of
finance, so it does not employ mercenary leg-breakers to
reclaim debts from: the: Exalted. Indeed, it doesn’t even
directly refuse to do business with the debtor; itwill extend
him lines of credit until Hoge 's accountants believe that

<!--PAGE 88-->
lines of further credit. This sort of treatment can destroy
an Exalt’s finances and reputation in a matter of just a
few days. While most Dragon-Blooded keep things from
going this far or fall into Ragara’s welcoming arms as
their own businesses start to collapse, some do run for
the Threshold, hoping to establish themselves well
away from the network of houses that they believe lured
them into trouble.

Ragara frequently sends unExalted family members as
auditors to visit its satrapies. These family members tour the
countryside and do an accounting of a province’s resources.
They carefully calculate exactly what they think the satrap
can afford while still allowing some room for the province to
grow and use that figure to determine their tribute. Many
satraps groan at the arrival of these auditors, knowing that
they'll be squeezed for everything they can afford. Others,
havingseen neighbors get far worse deals from their patrons,
are grateful that Ragara at least takes economic growth into
account. Ragara is good about hiring mercenaries to help
protect its investments, both from foreign threats and from
the satrap’s own greed. Because of this, most of their satraps
are still paying most, if not all, of their tribute.

GOALS & ALLIANCES

Ragara prefers to keep the other Great Houses in its
debt rather than ally itself with them, and this is one of the
few things standing in its way of the Scarlet Throne. This
policy worked well before, but it’s leaving Ragara behind
now. Banoba is considering trying to marry one of his own
sons off to V'neef Aliset but realizes he's probably a bit late.
He thinks he'll have a better chance with a young Tepet,
since Tepet is more in need of Ragara’s help right now.

Ragara doesn't get along with House Ledaal at all.
It has never been able to fathom the actions and
choices of Ledaal, and that makes Ledaal seem dangerous. Ragara has tried again and again to get Ledaal into
its debt, but somehow, Ledaal always seems to slip
through Ragara’s fingers.

Ragara Felis, a high-ranking patrician in the Thousand Scales, recently married Peleps Magaret, an inspired
Dragon-Blooded sorcerer 40 years his junior. The two are
very much in love, and their families took advantage of the
situation to cementan alliance between some of the Peleps
judicial influence and the Ragara Guild interests. The
Guild is always willing to trade goods and favors for judicial
rulings to its benefit.

Ragara Soras Tara acts as caretaker for old Ragara
himself right now. She oversees the servants who tend to

House Cynis (Woop)

Cynis herself has been dead almost two centuries.
House Cynis is run jointly by her three Exalted daughters:
Wisel, Belar and Falen. These three hedonistic Dragon-
Blooded have few quarrels over how to run the house. In
fact, they spend most of their time in the Palace of Trees
in Pangu Prefecture, hosting parties and orgies, overseeing
the day-to-day affairs of their family and taking turns
visiting the Imperial City to host parties there.


### Major Lines


All of House Cynis is descended from these three
daughters. Cynis only had two other children, both
unExalted daughters, and they died in their early 20s.
Rumor has it that Cynis was so upset they didn’t Exalt
that she drove them to suicide. Unkinder tongues say she
had them killed.

Outside of House Cynis, all members of the house
take Cynis’ name as their family name. Within the
house, all members of the house except the three daughters take the name of the daughter they’re descended
from as their family name. Although the daughters have
no qualms about allowing family members to intermarry, they do insist that intermarriage must only occur
between members of the different lines. It only took a
few generations before the blood was hopelessly mixed,
but the tradition remains.

to alienate the Guild — particularly with the number of

made it known to some of Cynis’ more pliable satraps that

of their peasants as slaves in its place.

Meanwhile, Wisel has started a new and little-known
family business. House Cynis has learned enough about
the Dynasts and patricians who attend its discreet orgies by
now to know which ones have “unnatural” tastes. The
Cynises now quietly offer certain services to their favorite
politicians and allies that would make the patrons very
unpopular if the general public found our. Thanks to their
location in rural Pangu, they have plenty of open land on
which to bury slaves’ bodies.

Cynis also likes to hire out its slaves to those who
need cheap labor. The unExalted aren’t allowed to keep
slaves, and the house is perfectly happy to make money
off of this fact. As long as the slaves are only on temporary
loan, technically, Cynis still owns them. The temporary
employer of these slaves makes a payment in jade to
House Cynis. In accordance with the laws of the Empress,
Cynis sends an overseer — usually a mortal but, occasionally, a young Exalt — in its own employ along with
any slaves that it lends out. It is ostensibly the responsibility of this overseer to manage the slaves’ daily activities,
but in many cases, the overseer allows the client hausehold to oversee the slaves as it wishes, while he enjoys the
client’s hospitality. The overseer’s main responsibility to
House Cynis, then, is to make certain that all slaves sent
out to aclient household return alive and in good health.
Those killed or maimed in working for the client are held
against the overseer’s stipend.

GOALs & ALLIANCES

Although the Cynises are very popular come party
time, there are many Dynasts who don’t want to be seen as
allied with them. This can make formal alliances and
public showings of support difficult for the house. This is
one of the reasons why Cynis fights so hard for the Scarlet
Throne. The Cynises know that if they’re going to stay in
areal position of power, they need to secure it now.

In the interest of this goal, Cynis is hurriedly setting. up.as many marriages as it can with other houses. This gets

Cynis Falen Debos inherited his great-greatgrandmother's cold practicality but loathes the hedonism
of his house. As soon as he graduated from the House of

House CATHAK (Fire)

Cathak Cainan, the elder of the house, is a greatgrandson of Cathak himself. The martial nature of House
Cathak causes a greater than average death rate among the
house’s Dragon-Blooded, and the founding members of
the house are long dead. Cainan, a surprisingly tall man
with shoulder-length fire-red hair, is proud and strong. He
walks with measured stride and a confident smile. He has
a strong influence over his house, but he doesn’t consider
himself its ruler or leader.

Cainan enjoys power but cares more for the strength
and power of the Realm than his own personal power. He
believes in the inherent right of the Dragon-Blooded to
rule and knows with certainty that they are the only ones
fit for the job. Cathak has a great deal of influence and
power to throw into the struggle for the Scarlet Throne,
and Cainan has yet to decide whose side will receive his
support. He feels that a worthy successor to the Empress
has not yet come forward, and he will not stand behind
someone who cannot lead the Realm into greatness.


### Major Lines


Cathak Garel, a military historian, formed her own
household in.Realm Year 542. The line of Garel has
produced many fine:historians as well as the traditional
Cathak contingent of-strategists and generals. The Garel
household, based in the Arjuf Dominion near the. city of
Tuchara, is: sai k ep the | most st complete and accurate

Economics

Cainan has a habit of squeezing the house’s satrapies
a bit hard. He sees it as fair recompense for the empire's
guidance and protection; many of the subject princes see
it somewhat less kindly. While Cathak has many legions,
they can’t be everywhere at once. Some of Cathak’s

Traditionally such schools charge moderate tuition

Cathak is currently negotiating a possible joint venture with House V'neef through V'neef Aliset. Aliset
wants backing and occasional military backup for a new
trade route she’s trying to establish with a couple of
isolated Threshold towns between Larjyn and Rathess
willing to supply unusual weapons, scavenged First Age
goods and odd foodstuffs. Cathak wants to make sure
Aliset can handle setting the whole thing up before it
commits any goods or men. Aliset has promised to arrange
for samples of the wares and a few Tepet boats to do the
actual shipping. She knows of a particularly promising
village, Poral, on the coast of Lake Carda, between Larjyn
and Rathess; rumors abound of First Age goods submerged
beneath the waters of that lake.

House Members OF NOTE

Cathak Mae makes a living in Tuchara as a master
armorer. Normally, such a career would be seen as beneath
one of her stature. However, she’s so good at what she does,
and has such high-ranking clients, that her house is quite
proud of her work. She gives a steep discount to family.
Mac is perfectly happy where she is and has no interest in
politics. That doesn’t stop members of her family from
trying to drag her into them, however. Cainan sees Mae’s
client list as a potential source of contacts. He tries not to
push the issue with her often, but when necessary, he calls
on her to make use of that resource.

Cathak Laten is a member of a sworn brotherhood
with V’neef Aliset and Tepet Darik. The three of them
together conceived of their joint shipping venture. Laten
hopes to convince his parents to make a bid for Aliset's
hand in marriage for him; he isn’t in love with her, but he’s
convinced she’d make a powerful wife. Laten is hotheaded
and impulsive but honorable and caring. His parents have
high hopes for his future.

‘THe DINNER COMPANIONS.

Cathak Laten, V’neef Alisetand Tepet Darik
have been friends since childhood. They attended primary school together at Golden Band

House NELLENS

their best to presenta unified front when dealing with the
other houses. Other members of the house are free to
agree or disagree with the committee as they see fit, but
as the members of the committee have a fair amount of
power between them, most Nellens put forward at least a
token effort to comply with rheir wishes.

Major Lines

Because Nellens has such a handicap when dealing
with the other houses, its members do their best to act as
one when they can. All Nellens households go to great
lengths to keep the Nellens name, and most of the family
lives together in the city of Juche. There are wo major
households that bear the Nellens name but do not live in
Juche; both were formed by patricians.

Nellens Kepel acted as the governor of Guin, a
farming community in Gedir Prefecture, for 40 years.
His daughter took over after him, and her daughter after
her, and even now, the household looks over the majority of this sprawling village and the surrounding environs.
The household is fairly provincial and a little secretive
and doesn’t tend to travel much, but it has given House
Nellens an economic foothold in the farming and livestock industries.

Nellens Chenault started a very successful shipping
company more than a century ago and formed an equally
successful household around the endeavor. This company
services various farming communities and small-scale
manufacturers around the Realm and ships through Arjuf,
Lord’s Crossing and Eagle’s Launch. The family uses the
opportunity to keep up contacts with many of the minor
lords and governors around the Blessed Isle.

Economics

House Nellens-has its fingers in a number of the
smaller industries ; around the Realm, areas that the
overlook. . These -include
, whaling expéditions,
or shipping’ and” trade

<!--PAGE 92-->
Nellens has very few satrapies under its control, but
this suits the house just fine. It'd rather stick to its many
small ways of making money. The house isn’t set up to deal
with large shipments of tribute and all of the attendant
difficulties that spring from it. In fact, rather than taking
tribute directly from those satrapies it does claim, Nellens
often makes complex deals instead. These result in the
house’s satraps shipping goods to entirely unrelated locales
where the Nellens have allies and friends. These allies and
friends then do other favors for the Nellens or pass the
house a safer and easier source of income or trade. It’s a
complex maze of connections and trades that leaves Nellens
not only in possession of more goods for less effort, but also
helps to keep its tributaries and satraps happy. The smaller
trades and shipments are not only cheaper, but easier to
protect. Such arrangements even managed to hide some of
Nellens’ income from the Empress, and Nellens elders are
certain they will have even more success concealing their
transactions from the Empress’ successor.

Nellens also goes to some lengths to make friends with
tributaries that are feeling squeezed by other houses. It has
secretly underwritten a handful of tribute payments in
return for favors of various sorts.

GOALS & ALLIANCES

The Nellens know that most of the Dragon-Blooded
loathe and despise them, so they prefer to work on the
patricians. UnExalted Dynasts, Exalted Dynasts, they really don’t care. They'll work with and fere almost anyone
who's capable of furthering their ambitions and helping
them to contend with the more powerful houses. Their
favored tactic is to make friends with unExalted Dynasts
who feel disillusioned with or betrayed by their houses.

Nellens desperately wants either the Throne ora very
firm grasp on the house or person who takes it. Unlikely as
that seems, Nellens expect it’s the only way itll remain a
viable house in the long run.

House Mempers OF NOTE

Nellens Mirar is one of the younger Dragon-Blooded
members of the house. While on the surface she is treated
as the spiritually superior being that the Immaculate
Philosophy deems her to be, she cannot help but sense a
certain resentment among her patrician siblings. She
does her best to ignore it and treat all of her family —
Exalted:and unExalted — with fairness, but she’s reaching. her: ne Her patrician Parents subely*pur-their

that milestone. Linus can be found at any party that admits
unExalted Dynasts, wining and dining potential contacts.
He has a network of informants that has impressed even
House Iselsi, and he hopes to parlay the use of that network
into permission to marry an Iselsi Exalted. He has some
idea of the Iselsi connection to the All-Seeing Eye and is
more interested in the information that could provide him
than he is in Ragara’s wealth or Cathak’s military power.

House Teret (Air)

Tepet has become fairly fractured in recent decades,
and the situation has only gotten worse since their legions
were destroyed battling barbarians in the North. Tepet
himself has been dead for some time, and his five Dragon-
Blooded children fight over the direction and influence of
the house. They have enough similar aims that the house
isn’t torn apart by their bickering, but they do keep it from
being as powerful as it could be.


### Major Lines


Tepet has six major family lines. Two of these were
started by elders, Marek and Vergus. Both are military
families. Grandchildren of Tepet started several other
households: Nerigus, Berel and Tilis. Nerigus and Berel are
both merchant families based out of Lord's Crossing; Tilis
is another military family.

The last of the lines was started by a recently
Exalted and wedded descendent of the house. Deramol
is unusual for the Tepets — he cares more about lazy
pursuits such as horse-breeding and hunting than military matters, and prefers social events to the heat of
battle. Ironically, the three military families, long the
more influential households within House Teper, lost
the best and brightest of their young Exalted in the
destruction of the Tepet legions. The other three households, although they lost a few of their children, survived
with much more of their resources intact.

EcONoMICcsS

Economically speaking, Tepet is in dire straits. It
collects few tributes and now has no means of enforcing
those tributes. It is unlikely that the house’s satrapies will
continue to send it anything now that its legions have been
destroyed and it lacks the money.to pay for mercenaries:

support the house in:

Goats & ALLIANCES

Tepet needs all of the alliances it can handle, but the
proud elders of the house don’t want to be seen as desperate. They have little choice, really. They're pulling in
every favor they can to partner up with other houses.
They’re sending every young house member they have left
out to find new opportunities and sources of income.
Notably, house elders have begun to lean on young Tepet
Darik to take advantage of his relationship with his bonded
circlemates Cathak Laten and V’neef Aliset to improve
the house’s financial and military straits.

Tepet Vergus has long been friends with V’neef,
and he’s calling on that friendship now. He wants a
hand up into what has recently become V’neef’s nighexclusive province: wine production and sales in the
Realm. V'neef hasn’t yet decided whether their friendship is worth such a price.

House MemBers OF NOTE

Tepet Locus recently graduated from the Heptagram.
His teachers have deemed him full of promise, and he has
an eagerness and energy to match. He’s slightly naive in
the ways of politics; he just never seemed to get the point
of betrayal and back-stabbing. He's faultlessly honorable
and unfailingly polite. He’s also head over heels in love
with Ledaal Sirel, another Exalt in his class. Preliminary
talks between his parents and hers were going well until
the Tepet legions were destroyed. Now, Sirel’s parents
won't even talk to Locus.’ Locus has sworn to find a way to
make marriage worthwhile for Sirel’s family before she’s
married off to someone else.

Tepet Deramol Kelles is just as idealistic as her
younger cousin but not nearly as naive. She’s an abolitionist and, publicly, a socialite who plays up her reputation as
shallow nobility at the many parties she attends. She finds
out which people abuse their slaves the most and works to
steal those servants away to the Threshold. Still, she
retains her practicality — she often returns to claim favors

from those she frees.

with 4n.aura of command about her. She holds the rank

House MNEMON (EARTH)

Mnemon should be the obvious choice for the Scarlet
Throne —- she’s extraordinarily powerful, very old and
experienced and, arguably, the next-most-powerful single
person in the Realm after her mother. This also explains
why no one wants her there — nobody would willingly
have elected the Scarlet Empress either. Someone who
already has that much power and isso farremoved from the
concerns of her lessers is hardly a popular choice for
Empress. Perhaps she could do a lot for the Realm, but it’s
also certain she’d put herself and her house first.

Mnemon is arrogant, frighteningly intelligent and as
publicly devout as she can manage. The Immaculate
Philosophy is what put her near the top of the totem pole,
and she’s determined to use it like rhe club it is. She has
an extraordinary talent for Charms and sorcery. While
the demands she makes on the abilities of her descendants are outrageous, she'll go to equally great lengths to
protect their reputations and positions. Her great-grandchildren are fond of saying that Mnemon expects her
family to kill themselves to please her every whim, but at
least they'll die at che top.

MAjor LINES

Almost all of House Mnemon is publicly devout.
Some emulate the Earth Dragon because it is expected of
them. Some keep up the practice because they realize that
the Immaculate Philosophy is what helps them stay in
charge. Still others — and a surprising number at that —
are truly devout. The family of Mnemon Caras number
themselves among the devout, and fully one-third of the
children of this family go on to become monks of the
Immaculate Order. Most of the rest attend the Cloister of
Wisdom and study both philosophy and the martial arts at
the knee of the Order. The descendants of Caras take
House Iselsi’s presence at the Palace Sublime as a personal
affront and long for the day when they can relocate the
center of the Order to the Imperial City.

Mnemon Darow married a Cathak general many
years ago, and the two of them started a household that has
since spawned many fine generals and strategists. It has
become something of a tradition for the eldest daughter of
each generation to marry a Cathak, keeping the military
ties: of the household strong.

disaster, however, Mnemon has been more cautious about
sending her legions outside of the Realm. Asa result, her
tributaries have become bolder about withholding tribute. The satraps have little love for the house — while it
has protected its tributaries in the past, it’s much more
cautious now, and yet, it hasn’t reduced the outrageous
tributes it expects.

House Mnemon has its fair share of merchants in the
family, and this has helped to keep the house from losing
too much money in the recent upheaval. Also, thanks ro
the house’s fervent emulation of Pasiap, there area number
of architects in the family. While the rate of construction
of new buildings has fallen off due to the recent economic
troubles, someone always needs a new house or temple
designed and built, and there are certainly plenty of
fortifications going up. House Mnemon sends a number of
its young patricians to study in the schools of Tuchara,
insisting on only the best education for its children.

GOALS & ALLIANCES

Mnemon has a habit of looking down on everyone —
even, some gossip, the Empress herself. She certainly
doesn't see her siblings’ children as her equals in any way
and has made no secret of this attitude in the past. This
arrogance is hurting hernow. What makes up for this is the
fact that House Mnemon is so powerful. No one can afford
to look like they don’t want Mnemon’s help.

Mnemon’s line breeds quite true, producing a prodigious number of Dragon-Blooded. Every single one of the
houses wants the benefit of that sort of pedigree, and
children of House Mnemon have no trouble finding suitors. This means that Mnemon has ended up with many
small alliances spread throughout the houses rather thana
few very strong ones. While this gives the house contacts
and aid from all quarters, it also means that none of that aid
is particularly powerful. The closest the house has to an
actual ally in this quarter is House Sesus, which particularly prides itself on marrying for Mnemon blood and
Cynis business sense.

Mnemon will not be satisfied with anything less than
the Throne. The thought of bowing down to any of her
lesser relatives is completely galling and violates everything Mnemon cherishes about the Immaculate Philosophy.
She is privately furious thatthe matter isn ‘talready settled

Mnemon Timaro, Nepel’s twin sister, seems the
exact opposite of Nepel — she’s quiet and a little shy, and
she seems to keep to the sidelines in any dispute. No one
can remember the last time she publicly chose sides

House Sesus (Fire)

House Sesus is comprised of five separate family
lines; the eldest couples of each line gather to make
decisions for the house as a whole. The first line was
started by Sesus Kajak, a famous general and the eldest
son of Sesus himself. It is now run by Sesus Kajak Raves
and her husband, Cynis Nesil. Once a proud military
household, it now holds more interest in mercantile
affairs. While it still bankrolls quite a number of troops,
it mostly uses them as a source of income, loaning them
out inside the family to browbeat satraps. The line still
produces the occasional brilliant general, however.

The second line was started by Sesus Alon, a powerful
senator and a granddaughter of Sesus. It has remained a
very political family; the resources of its sister families give
it a great deal of leverage to work with. It is currently lead
by Sesus Alon and her husband, Mnemon Barin. Alon is
a cautious player, who researches political situations thoroughly before casting her vote.

The third family line was started by Sesus Magel, a
politician whose husband, Cynis Parovar, was a patrician
merchant with a keen and coldly calculating business
acumen. The current head of the household is Sesus
Magel Talor, a widowed politician thoroughly and happily in the pocket of the Guild. He has recently taken a
secret peasant lover in an attempt to deal with the
loneliness created by his wife’s death.

Sesus Chenow was a brutal adventurer who enjoyed
killing and pillaging to such an extent that he became a
«political liability. When the family threatened to disown
“him; Chenow pulled himself together and started his own
~“military household. It soon became apparent. that he’d
done so in order to have the means to ‘carry on his
brutalities in distant Threshold lands instead, but this

didn’t make the house look bad, so Sesus let hity have his.”

youngest daughter, Sesus Chenow Mareq, runs the household with her husband, Cathak Catis.

The final household was started by Sesus Denerid, a
rebellious youth with a great love of Cynis parties. The
Denerid household maintains the house's “legion” of
slave concubines and has a long-term contract for slaves
with House Cynis. It has become traditional for the
Denerid household to intermarry extensively with Cynis,
and some consider the household to be almost as much a
part of House Cynis as it is a part of House Sesus. It is
currently controlled by Sesus Denerid Gutar and his
patrician wife, Cynis Violas.


### Economics


House Sesus has a pretty good reputation with its
satraps — it hasn’t insisted on huge tributes, and it has
occasionally ler shortfalls slide for a year if matters have
gone badly for the tributary in question. This leniency is
proving half-useful for Sesus now, as its tributaries have no
real grudges to bear. On the other hand, Sesus is trying to
tighten its grasp now; it needs every bit of tribute its
satrapies can spare. Its satraptes, however, aren’t used to
enforcing these sorts of demands, and the subject queens
and princes are resisting itas best they can. So far, Chenow’s
troops have been able to keep things under control, but
there’s only so much they can do. Things have already
started slipping, and there’s rumor that one of the family’s
tributaries —- either Kernig or Valis, both woodcutters’
strongholds in the East — might try to revolt.

Sesus has profited well off of Cynis’ commercial acumen through extensive intermarriage. The patricians of
House Sesus have their fingers in all sorts of middling
commercial ventures around the Realm. These ventures
include two textile factories in Incas Prefecture, a siakahunting interest in Eagle’s Launch, a mining group in
Blackwater and a physicians’ cooperative in the Imperial
City. Unfortunately, the house has concentrated on military might toan extent that has prevented it from acquiring
any major commercial ventures to keep it profitable in the
current times. So while its members aren't starving, neither are their coffers expanding.

GOALS & ALLIANCES

Sesus prides itself on its breeding and has concentrated on wooing ‘Cynis and Mnemon almost to the
exclusion of some of the other houses. This gives ita high
rate of Dragoni-Blooded births, some practical commercial
know- how.an te*for: sivss and t hedonism, but it

<!--PAGE 96-->
House Iselsi has made quiet noises about helping
Sesus get a leg up over Mnemon if Sesus will guarantee that
the Immaculate Order will remain centered at the Palace
Sublime. Sesus still hopes to work something out with
Mnemon or Cynis, so it hasn’t given Iselsi an answer yet;
if Mnemon does take the Throne, Sesus certainly doesn’t
want her to find out that it bargained with the Iselsi.
However, Sesus would rather see anyone else on the
Throne than Mnemon, despite their ties; Sesus knows
intermarriage won’t count for much once Mnemon has
control. The house is still hoping that the future ruler will
either be a Sesus or a Cynis, and it may need to deal with
Iselsi to have a chance at that.

House MEMBERS OF NOTE

Sesus Chenow has officially retired to an undisclosed
location in the Western Isles. Unofficially, he has pulled
his old sworn brotherhood back together again and gone
out for another few rounds of vicious war and bloodshed.
He swore in his youth that he’d die fighting, and he isn’t
about to let old age creep up on him and deny him that
tight. He’s just past his prime and barely starting to go gray.
As soon as he developed his first few wrinkles, he retired
with barely a goodbye, secretly afraid that if he waited any
longer he wouldn’t be in any shape to go back to his old
ways. Chenow and his friends have made it their personal
mission to keep Threshold tributaries paying their tribute
in full and on time, particularly those that belong to House
Sesus. They might succeed, but they're certainly making
some enemies along the way.

Sesus Geroc is young, nice and recently graduated
from the House of Bells. He is also in the unfortunate
position of being Chenow’s chosen protégé. Geroc is a bit
impulsive, and the aging Chenow decided that he could
shape this young man into his successor. He’s sure he can
rectify the boy’s unfortunate lack of brutality by showing
him how barbaric and ungrateful the house’s subject queens
and princes are. Along the way, Geroc is seeing more than
he ever wanted to of poverty, degradation and violence.
He's fiercely loyal to his house, but recently, he’s entertained some very unloyal thoughts toward Chenow, and
he’s close to the breaking point.

House Iseis) (WATER)

House Iselsi cannot realistically be said to have a
ruler oft any kind. No committee guides it. N

travel incognito. They have no chance whatsoever in
the battle for the Scarlet Throne. Their only hope is to
get someone in power that doesn’t want to exterminate
them or move the Palace Sublime into the Imperial
City. The house has no voice in the Deliberative and
previously subsisted on the favor of the Empress and the
Bronze faction, working as spies and assassins for the
Throne and the Immaculate Order. Its reputation has
been destroyed, and the other houses see Iselsi as a pawn
at best and a bug to be squashed underfoot at worst.

Major LINES

Rather than a handful of major bloodlines, Iselsi has
been broken up beyond recognition at this point. Before
the house fled in disgrace, there were considered to be
three major households. The first was started by Iselsi
Yamah. It concentrated its efforts on the All-Seeing Eye
and indulged in some less-than-popular activities such as
assassination, weapons sales and shipments to Threshold
tributaries and spying on anyone and everyone. Iselsi
sacrificed much of its influence to retain its positions in
the All-Seeing Eye. This part of the house was long ago
made subject to the powers that controlled the Realm,
but it managed to retain its identity and some influence
as an agent of the Realm. Not a single one will be found
living openly as an Iselsi. Some of the family’s more
distant lines have already forgotten that they are descended from a Dynastic house.

The second household was created by Iselsi Manoton
and had a distinctly military bent. Unfortunately for
Iselsi, this household was fairly young and had not yet
built up much in the way of troops. This household took
the brunt of the repercussions from the attempted assassination attempt against the Empress in Realm Year
303. Most of them died in the coup attempt, the ill-fated
campaign that led up to it and defending the retreat of
the imperial forces from the Scavenger Lands. This
household has vanished into obscurity.

The third line, created by Iselsi Savenis, preferred
to have its hands into anything that furthered the
house’s pursuit of knowledge. It too made use of the
All-Seeing Eye, but it also sent sworn brotherhoods
out into the Threshold to learn everything possible
about the cultures and provinces there. Many descen:.
dants of this line still: livein the Threshold; and there. :

<!--PAGE 97-->
Wuy Do THE JSELSI SURVIVE?

Given that they have been disgraced for centuries, left powerless and unable to defend
themselves, why is it that the Iselsi persevere while
Great Houses rise and fall? Part of the answer is that
the Empress wanted them to. They are a lesson —
offend the Scarlet Empress, and your grandchildren’s
children will live in disgrace.

The dismemberment of House Iselsi has been
a long and painful process, an ongoing object
lesson for the other Great Houses about the rewards of treachery. Though the ill-fated coup
attempted happened shortly after the disastrous
campaign of RY 301, it was almost two and a half
centuries later than the Empress formally dissolved the house. In RY 570, the Empress formally
ordered House Iselsi struck from the imperial
ledgers, through she had slowly been stripping it
of satrapies and privileges for all the intervening
years. No further members were named to the
Deliberative, and the last Iselsi member of the
Deliberative died in RY 696.

The other reason was that the Iselsi disgrace
wasn’t as complete as the imperial government
implied. The Empress didn’t annihilate the house,
she appropriated it. Many of those “executed” and
“imprisoned for life” were given new faces and
identities through sorcery and bureaucratic footwork and used as Dragon-Blooded agents of the
All-Seeing Eye, with their remaining relatives
held hostage to their good performance. For centuries, chey were among the most reliable assassins
and agents of the Scarlet Throne, Dragon-Blooded
spymasters and killers unable to enter normal
society and willing to do anything to preserve
their lineage. All were fully aware of the fact that
the if the Empress fell from power, their chances
were nonexistent. Now that she is gone, the
wolves are closing in, and the Iselsi are desper- .
ately seeking a way to preserve their house against
even these impossible odds.

While the Great Houses always knew this was
happening, the legal pretexts made it difficult to
tell who actually died and who was made an agent.
This is one of the reasons the Great Houses
haven’t moved jointly to smash Iselsi yet. Not
only do Bal Keraz and the elders of the immaculate Order support the house, nobody knows who
might actually be an Iselsi agent — and what
terrible reprisals they might exact if misfortune
were to strike the remnants of the family.

<!--PAGE 98-->
Rireegs

Economics

Iselsi is dying, fiscally as well as numerically. It
might be a long, slow death, but it is a death nonetheless. Iselsi has no voice in the Deliberative. It has no
legions, and it has no satrapies under its control. It
cannot openly delve into commercial concerns, so all of
its commercial ventures must, of necessity, be small and
subtle. The house cannot risk catching the attention of
its distant cousins, and since its members cannot openly
intermarry with the other houses, its blood becomes
more diluted with every generation,

Iselsi bolsters its flagging coffers by becoming involved with various religious communities around the
Realm, but few of these communities are wealthy, and
this tactic is a delaying one at best. Iselsi has a firm
presence, as well as political support and some money, in
the towns of Ashra, Guin, Noble and Spider’s Crossing.
It has moved spies and other operatives into Nishimo,
Cloaka and Traiter, as well as a few other small communities, but it must move slowly.

GOALS & ALLIANCES

The elders of House Iselsi currently split their efforts
in several different directions. They'd like to influence the
battle for the Scarlet Throne. Their most important goal
is to see that Mnemon doesn’t take the Throne, and
they’re prepared to do almost anything to see to that — up
to and including assassination, if it comes down to that
(and if they can pull it off, which is unlikely). Mnemon
would certainly move the Immaculate Order within the
Imperial City, which would send Iselsi scurrying for cover
again and ruin any chance of the house pulling itself
together. Iselsi’s current favored house is Sesus; Sesus has
strong ties to Mnemon but is ultimately self-serving. Sesus
also stands a relatively strong chance of taking the Throne
and has enough jaded tastes that Iselsi might be able to get
a handle on one of his vices. The house elders second and
third choices are Ledaal and Tepet, but neither house is
likely to take the Throne and, Iselsi knows this. It would
rather bet on a winner.

Iselsi has lately debated the wisdom of moving the
remaining center of the house to the Threshold. It seems
as though every year the concentration of Mnemons in the
Immactilate Order grows. While the Mouth of Peace
iving Iselsi sanctuary as the policies of the Order
ret allies among the Sidereals) dictatesic.

fact that the Empress could have easily ordered the house
liquidated. Now, with the Empress gone, Iselsi is hoping to
marry some of its people back into the Dynastic houses
and, thus, work them into truly influential positions again.
It’s a risky gambit, but Iselsi has little left to lose.

House Members OF NOTE

Whitemane Vocal is the patrician governor of the
town of Spider’s Crossing. His father is one of the few
remaining Iselsi elders, and he’s completely loyal to the
house. Vocal projects an image of a relaxed, calm, completely provincial governor. He has a strong local accent
(exaggerated) and uses his reputation for approaching
everything slowly, thoroughly and seriously to gather
information for the house. He's known as an honorable
and trustworthy man, and he considers that reputation his
most valuable asset. Vocal carefully balances the maintenance of that reputation with his desire to feed all
information to the house. Vocal is descended from the
Yamah line of House Iselsi.

Blackwing is a Dragon-Blooded who emulates the
Water Dragon. He comes from a minor patrician family
and revels in acting as one of the black sheep of the
Dragon-Blooded of Iselsi. His mother was an unExalted
Iselsi, but she carefully doctored her husband's family
records to imply that a Dragon-Blooded had sired the
child. While this still leaves Blackwing to be looked down
upon by other Exalted as a non-Dynastic patrician, it at
least keeps the Dynasts from looking too carefully into his
parentage. He is strong-willed and brash and loves to crash
Dragon-Blooded parties that he hasn’t been invited to.
This habit has earned him some enemies and gotten him
beaten up a few times, but it has also made him a couple of
friends among the more rebellious Dragon-Blooded youth.
Blackwing doesn’t yet know of his parentage, although his
mother has tried to subtly teach him that House Iselsi has
been treated unfairly by the Realm.

student of history like many of his family. He spent most
of his life searching out lose historical texts and. studying

knowledge and experience are too valuable at home.

Ledaal Caros is a strategist. He studied (and has
taught) at the House of Bells, and has advised several of the
Realm’s great generals. The house's Sidereal advisors predicted his worth as a strategist at his birth, and so, he has
never been allowed to take his own course in life. He has
suppressed his frustration at this, as he understands what is
at stake. As a result, he has gradually become more and
more morose and peevish throughout the years.

Ledaal Cycel has an eidetic memory and an uncanny
ability to figure out who will do the most good where.
Because of this, she runs most of the day-to-day operations
of the house and often consults with the Sidereals on
which children should be encouraged to go into what
fields. She has no compunctions about pressuring parents
into dictating their children’s career choices.


### Major Lines


There are only two major lines of House Ledaal. The
first was started by Ledaal Catala and is based in the eastern
forests of the Threshold rather than in the Realm. This
branch of the family finds it easier to gather information
and items in the Threshold without being noticed when its
members don’t have to travel back and forth to the Realm
‘all the time. They concentrate on studying the Deathlords,
the shadowlands and the Anathema. They collect old
texts and new intelligence, ferret out foreign business
opportunities to keep the house afloat and make friends
andallies in places where much of the empire fears to tread.

Ledaal Kebok is a scholar and sorcerer, and he still
teaches occasionally at the Heptagram. His descendants
collect historical texts and record oral histories, study
sorcery at the knee of the Sidereals and run a decent
information-gathering network with eyes and ears in most
of the major cities around the Realm. Rumor has it that
one of Kebok’s eldest daughters, Sulco, took a demon as
lover and that a strain of demon-blood taints his family
line. This rumor is true, although Kebok firmly denies it.

Economics

~ Most of Ledaal’s funding comes from its.control of
several major southern ports, periculer ys Agiut This

any tributary prince with experience knows to fear
House Ledaal. Ledaal doesn’t squeeze every talent itcan
out of its tributaries, but ic accepts no excuses either.
Anyone who doesn’t pay up on time and in full can
expect his province to experience mysterious troubles
— droughts, floods, earthquakes, plagues and spirit
infestations of the worst kind.

GOALs & ALLIANCES

Ledaal has no interest in governing the Realm, but
it has every interest in maintaining the empire. To that
end, it is willing to make deals that help others get their
chance at the Scarlet Throne in return for money,
information, old texts and help against the Deathlords.
Their choice for ruler would be a Cathak — they expect
that the Cathak’s military nature and lust for conquest
would lead it to do something about the Deathlords
where other houses might not.

Ledaal hunts down rhe Anathema more vigorously
than any other house, and because of this, as well as its
Sidereal connections, it gets along well with the immaculate Order. Occasionally, the Mouth of Peace will
send a monk or two to accompany house members on
expeditions of particular importance to the Order, and
Ledaal does the same in return. The only thing that keeps
the Order and Ledaal from getting along perfectly is
Ledaal’s tendency to hoard items and texts it finds rather
than giving them to the Order.

Ledaal makes all of the marriage-based alliances it
can, in the hopes that its members can draw other houses
into the fight against the Deathlords. So far, Ledaal is the
only house that acknowledges how serious the situation is,
and it wants to change that. Thus, Ledaal is quite happy
with the other houses’ rush to obtain alliances for their
efforts to take the Scarlet Throne. They're currently trying
to arrange several marriages with Cathak.

House MemBers OF NOTE

Ledaal Kebok Coren makes even her own siblings
nervous. Though she is newly graduated from the House
of Bells, her skill with a sword is prodigious, and her
mastery of Essence impressive for one so young. Her eyes
are the color of the sky, changing color with her mood
from clear bright*blue to the roiling darkness of storm
clouds. And as her:mood chanets, so does the weather

<!--PAGE 100-->
strong interest in her descendant’s doings, and it is
possible that Yan has as well.

Ledaal Ricot graduated from the Cloister of Wisdom
less than a decade ago, and his family held high hopes for
his promise as a scholar and politician who could look after
his family’s interests in the political realm. Unfortunately
for them, Ricot had other ideas and wasn’t willing to
buckle to pressure. Ricot is far more interested in adventure, parties and beautiful partners than he is in any sort of
political career. Maybe someday, he tells them, he'll settle
down, but he hopes that day won't come any time soon.
His family is trying to make up for his unexpected break
from its plans for him by arranging a marriage to someone
with strong political influence.

House Peters (WATER)

The Peleps are as slippery and tricky as the element
they emulate. The husband-and-wife team that currently
runs the house embodies these traits. Peleps Febaris and
her husband, Peleps Taxin, are very distant cousins and
both Dragon-Blooded. Both are politicians, and each has
been a member of the Deliberative. They have many
children, each one raised in an atmosphere of harsh
competition; trickery and back-stabbing are encouraged
as long as the house isn’t harmed. Febaris and Taxin
believe that this is the best way to train family members
for a life of politics.

This attitude has trickled down to many of the Peleps
families. In some branches of the family, competition is
more friendly; in others, it’s vicious and cutthroat. Competition is never supposed to be to the detriment of the
family, but sometimes, such problems are hard to avoid.
Few Peleps truly trust each other; for this reason, most
Peleps prefer to marry completely outside the family.
Other houses might use marriages to spy on the family,
after all, but at least they’re likely to be a little less open
about it. Most Peleps are fanatically loyal to the house but
would ruin each other’s plans in a heartbeat.


### Major Lines


Because of the cutthroat nature of the family, independent households come and go all the time. They rise
up defiantly only to be cut down by their siblings. Two
major lines have managed to survive through all of this
infighting, but a handful of minor ones can be found at
any gi ven Hime.

unExalted than most Dynastic households, but this has
allowed the household to ensconce itself thoroughly in
the Thousand Scales.

Three of the more recent minor households have
been started by Yolan, Calut and Deham. Yolan is a highranking officer in the imperial navy, as is her unExalted
husband. Calut is a powerful politician with a great ambition to see his house win the Scarlet Throne. Deham is a
monk who believes that the Immaculate Order should
take a greater role in the political arena of the Realm.

Economics

House Peleps makes its money in two ways: from the
support it receives for maintaining the imperial navy,
which Peleps commands, and from its political endeavors
and constant maneuverings with and against the other
houses. The Peleps are constantly offering political and
legal aid to other houses in return for political support,
joint investments and marriage arrangements.

The Peleps also make a fair amount of money off of
staka- and whale-hunting endeavors in the northern isles
where they make their home and from their tributaries,
which they squeeze mercilessly. So far, they’ve kept the
tribute coming by using the navy to threaten their tributaries. It’s only a matter of time before this threat isn’t
enough, however, and Peleps may soon go beyond mere
saber rattling and land mercenaries or a loyal legion to
keep the locals in line. If it does not, then house elders
fear a revolt within the year.

Calut’s household has brought a surprising amount of
money into the house so far. The household attributes this
to its members keen business sense and willingness to
invest in Threshold provinces and businesses. In reality,
they accomplish this through black-market sales of weapons, food and resources overseas. They’ve so far managed
to avoid retribution from the Guild by blatant use of force,
but the leaders of the Guild can’t be very happy about it.

GOALS & ALLIANCES

Peleps alliances and rivalries tend to be short-lived.
Most Peleps see little point in long-term alliances; the
political necessities that spur alliances rarely last that long,
after all. They make deals-as the need strikes them. The
house sets a fair store by honoring its word, however, and.
alliances, if made with: promi and’oaths, will be: kept.”

Houst MEMBERS OF NOTE

Peleps Magaret graduated the Heptagram three years
ago. When she went in, she was a lively, ambitious girl.
When she came out, she was thin, ethereal, almost wasted.
Half the time, she seems to be lost in her own head, and

an inspired young sorcerer, but her parents just worry.
They married her off as quickly as possible to the patrician

is many decades older than she; she behaves as aloving and
devoted wife. The only time that he doesn't get her full
attention is when she locks herself away for days at a time
to study and practice her sorcery.

Peleps Howit is a powerful unExalted judge in his 50s.
He enjoys his power and particularly enjoys the fact that
his Dragon-Blooded relatives rely on it. He likes making
the Exalted Dynasts run around trying to please him so that
he’ll help them, while he gravely plays the respectful
mortal just trying to aid his betters. He’s a scheming
bastard with a very. quick mind whose only weakness is
Cynis orgies. He’s wasted quite a bit of what he’s gained
just to gain entry to a couple of these events.
