of privileges, such as playtime, course selection or sweets at
meals. The most severe penalty schools mete out, however, is
expulsion, and this punishment is reserved for offenses such
as cheating or grievously injuring another student.

The most important and commonly used weapon in
the disciplinary arsenal, however, is humiliation. Embartassing a student in front of her peers — calling on her
repeatedly when she clearly has not done her homework,
posting poor examination results, making her stand before
the other students for an entire class — has a powerful
effect, and teachers are well aware of it. The exercise yard
competition does not escape their notice, and they are
quite willing to use it to their advantage. Resistinga caning
is a mark of honor among the children, after all; it gives
them a badge of pride to hold onto if they endured. Bur
there is no defense against humiliation, so long as a child
cares what her peers think of her.


### Secondary School


There are only four secondary schools within the
Realm, and they are prestigious, elite and reserved for
those who combine the talent and the resources to secure
entry for themselves. Fewer than 20 percent of the students
graduating from primary school in any given year go on to
secondary school, and approximately 90 percent of those


### Schools for the Incorrigible


There are a pair of schools on the Blessed Isle
that cater to students that no other school will
deal with. The House of Ancient Stone, in Lord’s
Crossing, and the Palace of the Tamed Storm, in
the Imperial City, both take the children that
other schools can’t or won’t any more. These two
schools are both built more like prisons than
academics, and they are run with an emphasis on
the whip. By sending her child to one of these
schools, a parent is admitting that her child is out
of control and needs a sterner hand.

Graduates of these schools tend to come in
three flavors. Those who adjust to harsh discipline
almost uniformly excel later in life, and magistrates,
generals and other luminaries who bear the mark of
one of these schoolsare feared and respected. Those
whoat the first taste of the lash learn to behave out
of the simple desire to go unnoticed are generally
broken by the process and rarely venture beyond
low-level ministry posts or family estates.

And every year, some of the students leave
the House of Ancient Stone and the Palace of
the Tamed Storm ceremonially garbed for their
own funerals. Such is the risk of sending one’s
child to the most feared schools in the Realm.

<!--PAGE 134-->
are Dragon-Blooded. Unsurprisingly, most Dragon-Bloods
go on to secondary school. Those who do not drift into the
lower reaches of the legions and the Immaculate Order or
drift off to tend their family estates and trades.

The secondary schools provide a more focused education than do the primary schools. Each of the four academies
has a specialty, a core curriculum around which the school
is centered. Graduates are highly polished professionals
ready to step into their chosen fields and contribute
immediately, and great things are expected of them.

THE Logistics

Entering secondary school isa highly formal event, and
there are certain conventions to be obeyed and tests that
must be overcome. Talent matters here — imperial connections will barely suffice to buy your child's place in one of
these schools, and absolutely nothing will buy good marks.


### Acceptance


The process by which students are accepted to the four
academies is convoluted and lengthy. There is a formal
application process, during which the potential student’s
dominie, her teachers, her family and at least one reputable
witness from outside the family proper must vouch for her.
This, however, is only the start of the process. It merely
establishes the student as someone worthy of consideration.
What follows is a blur of interviews, examinations, underthe-table handoffs of cash and other favors and ruthless
attempts by parents to sabotage the candidacies of other
students. It is not enough that a child be qualified to enter
an academy or that she be driven to excel at the school’s
specialties. It must also be demonstrated to the dominie’s
satisfaction that having the child as a student will also
benefit the school. Usually, the benefit is measured by the
size of the donation the prospect's parents will make, but on
occasion, it also includes how much credit the child will
bring to the school in the long run.


### Maintenance


Unlike the primary schools, the four secondary academies do not charge tuition or other fees. Instead, they expect
donations, and the potential for substantial financial support
is one of the criteria for'a student’s admission. Parents are
expected to donate heavily: in honor of their: daughter's

<!--PAGE 135-->
Finally, each school save the Heptagram has an official patron. Funds from the Scarlet Throne flow into the
Spiral Academy and the House of Bells, and the Immaculate Order sees to the endowment of the Cloister of
Wisdom. Officially, the Heptagram has no sponsor, though
many believe this is just a smokescreen. Theories fly fast
and furious (a popular one among alumni of the House of
Bells is that Anathema actually run the school for their
own nefarious ends), but until such time as the school’s
anonymous backers reveal themselves — if they exist —
these theories will remain just that — theories.

THE Four ACADEMIES

The foursecondary schools on the Blessed Isle each feed
a different sector of the empire’s society. The House of Bells
caters to the military, the Spiral Academy to the bureaucracy, the Cloister of Wisdom to the Immaculate Order, and
the Heptagram to the need for magic and sorcery. Each is
relatively small; the largest has no more than 240 students,
and the smallest 80. Instruction is intense and often on an
individual basis, and the workload for each student is
monstrous. Many students wash out of the academies,
unable to cope with the brutal pace and fierce competition.


### The Cloister of Wisdom


A relatively small percentage of the students of the
Cloister of Wisdom ever join the Immaculate Order, but
all who study there benefit from the Immaculates’ wisdom,
experience and ability to train their students in new
patterns of thought. In terms of the number of students
enrolled, the Cloister is the second-smallest of the four
academies, and it tends to let prospective students know
they have been selected before they even think to apply.

The students at the Cloister are trained, first and
foremost, in meditation and ways to manipulate Essence.
Allelse, the staff believes, flows from these two. Ifastudent
can learn to master herself and master Essence, any other
task in life can be accomplished by combining those two
skills. That is not to say more mundane topics, such as the
Immaculate Texts, mathematics and rhetoric are neglected,
but rather, that they are viewed as adjuncts to the two core.
precepts. Students at the Cloister are actually.expected to

adem es, as the theory is that their base-grotinding in
hese important techniques should enable thém.to learn
all other skills more quickly. :

poet, playwright or martial arti
invitations are both extremel

The Cloister is located near the Palace Sublime in
Incas Prefecture. Like the Palace Sublime, the Cloister of
Wisdom is utterly indefensible, though no enemy would
dare attack the school. The reputation of its martial arts
instructors alone is enough to convince any earthly enemy
to seek an easier target.

THe Houst oF BELLS

The House of Bells teaches the arts of war. [ts curriculum
is designed around training the finest officers for the legions
and, to a lesser extent, the imperial navy. As such, its classes
focus on tactics, strategy, military history and logistics. Afternoons in the exercise yard are spent in training in both
unarmed and armed combat, maneuvers, battlefield sorcery
and endurance. The sixth and seventh day of every week are
devoted to recreating battles on the small scale, with older
students (calledcadets) maneuveringand ordering the younger
ones. Wooden weapons are supplied for these encounters, so
that the combat is appropriately fierce. Several times a year,
locals are called in to act as soldiers when larger battles are
reenacted, something which the school’s extensive grounds
and deep pockets make possible.

Located near the city of Arjuf, the House of Bells
sprawls over several leagues. Its grounds include fields, two
villages, a stretch of shoreline where amphibious assaults
and naval combatare practiced andastretch of rocky terrain
that is often used to simulate battles. A low stone wall
separates the House of Bells’ grounds from the rest of the
Realm, and the wall is pierced at five points with gates that
allow students, supplies and so forth to enter. Students are

“not allowed to leave the grounds save by special permission

of the dominie, and even then, those who request such
tter.tounded than their counterparts at ‘the other.

<!--PAGE 136-->
and days spent in the wilderness without tools or weapons
are all part of every student’s experience, and some simply
cannot handle it. Students are wounded or killed with some
regularity, and there is a high dropout rate. Those who fail
out of the House of Bells are barred by unwritten custom
from ever holding an officer’s post in the imperial legions.

Students at the House of Bells sleep in barracks. There
isno individual space; all is dedicated to the success or failure
of the group. Cadets are identified by year, and often, years
are pitted against one another in competitions. Within each
class, students are divided into individual squads, called
scales in imitation of the legion unit. Each scale is only as
strong as its weakest member, and they are graded on the
performance of the whole. This creates tremendous pressure
within the scales to raise everyone’s performance — and
sometimes results in weak links being “accidentally” injured
so that a replacement can be called in.

When students graduate from the House of Bells, they
generally acquire commissions in the legions or in private
armies across the Realm immediately. A House of Bells
graduate in good standing is welcomed in any army and
given respect in a way that few new recruits are.


### The Spiral Academy


The Spiral Academy is where ministers and bureaucrats are born. An academic sweatshop par excellence,
Students are relentlessly driven to excel at accounting,
finance, oration, calligraphy and all of the other arts
necessary to survive in the Imperial Bureaucracy. Poetry
appreciation is taught here, the better for a student to be
able to quote appropriate phrases in debate, but swordplay
is not much favored.

Students entering the Spiral Academy lose all contact
with the outside world for the duration of their study, with
the exception of the never-ending flow of reports about rice
production, shipping losses and the million other details
that make up the business of the Realm. While the staff
encourages apprenticeships with local ministers, most students find they learn more on the school grounds, drilled
endlessly by ministerial taskmasters in the minutiae of
commerce and record-keeping. Much of the work done in
the classrooms is actual bookkeeping, either mirroring the
work of others or (for older students) actually serving
purposes outside the school walls.

Here, more than in any other school, competition
between students is fierce. However, the Spiral Academy
also builds the strongest student and alumni networks of
any of the four academies. This is fueled in part by the low
esteem in which graduates of the other three hold the
bloodless, pen-pushing, bean-counting products of the
Academy, and no doubt, this attitude has also fueled the
antagonism between the branches of the Thousand Scales.

The Spiral Academy is physically the second-smallest
of the four schools, though the largest in terms of enrollment. Its enclosure is walled, and its buildings are high,
secure and heavily guarded. Courses are taught morning and
afternoon, with nights reserved for intensive study. Teachersat the Spiral Academy are most often retired ministers or
magistrates, but the Academy will pluck anyone it regards as
potentially useful and st id them upin front ofa classroom.

passed through the Academy's doors to make continued
support for it, even in these times, a forgone conclusion.


### The Heptagram


Located on a small, rocky island off the Blessed Isle’s
north coast, the Heptagram consists of a single tower and a
few outbuildings. Home tono more than 80studentsatatime,
it is the finest academy of magic, alchemy, demonology and
divination in the Realm, and it guards its secrets jealously.
Bound demons patrol the sea lanes leading to the island and
prevent unauthorized landings. Those who seek to visit the
Heptagram without permission end up feeding the crawling
things at the bottom of the sea with their corpses.

With nearly 40 teachers on staff, the Heptagram
provides the most personal education of any of the academies. However, with instruction in demon summoning
and the like, they also produce a higher rate of student
casualties than even the House of Bells. Early on in their
studies, students apprentice to a particular instructor and
follow in her footsteps for the duration of their stay.

Students at the Heptagram sleep two to a room, and
their quarters are relatively luxurious. Much of their time
is spent in study in one of the school’s libraries, of which
there are seven. There is less classroom time and more lab
work and independent study at the Heptagram, albeit all
under the watchful eye of at least one instructor.

This does not mean, though, chat more material
teachings are neglected. Even the most powerful sorcerer
is in a great deal of trouble if he can’t function in a social
setting, and thus, the importance of etiquette, dancing,
mathematics, composition and so on is stressed heavily.
The focus of the Heptagram remains firmly on magic,
though, and from the dullest student to the dominie
herself, all who dwell there are quite satisfied with this fact.


### Secret Societies


Even the elite have their elite, and in the secondary
schools those elite belong to the secret societies. Even the
Cloister has its share, and each school's societies are
unique to that school alone.

Iris the:most senior students in the society who select

### The Servants


The Heptagram is a dangerous place to work.
Assuch, most of the servants are golems, automata,
bound spirits or bound demons, as they're less
fragile. And with student sorcerers causing the
occasional catastrophe, having staff that doesn’t
require frequent replacement is a good thing.

When visitors come to the school, however,
great care is taken to hide the more bizarre laborersfrom the public eye. While the previousdominie
thought it did the school’s image good to put the
more obvious tokens of its power front and center,
the present one favorsa more subtle approach and
saves the shocks for suitable moments.

Each secret society has a different identifying mark by
which members can identify each other later in life. Often,
the societies cut across aspect and house lines, and as such,
they unintentionally do a great deal of work to unify the
Realm. After all, a man is born into his house and Exalted
by his blood, but he is chosen by a secret society, and that
makes all the difference.


### Sorcery Among the Dragon-blooded


Sorcerers are rare, even among the Dragon-Blooded.
Sorcery is dangerous and requires extensive study. Even
those who study sorcery over the course of many decades are
unlikely to learn more than a dozen spells. Since sorcerers
are sounusual, they hardly go unnoticed in Dynastic society.
They learn a great deal of occult knowledge, much of it
deliberately hidden from the eyes of society. They find that
they are outcasts within most nobles houses. Their lives are
not at all like those of their non-sorcerous peers, but they
still play an important role in the Realm.


### Education


Most sorcerers learn their trade at the Heptagram, the
secondary school for sorcerers on the Isle of Voices. The

<!--PAGE 138-->
; knowledge necessar}
_SOIcerers particularly fini

Sorcerers learn the rules of known spirit courts and
general rules that can be applied to many spirit courts.
They learn how to deal with these creatures, how to
command them, how to convince them and how to handle
them when they cause trouble. They learn of the Charms
different spirits might display and make use of. They learn
what knowledge the Dragon-Blooded possess about demons — their various circles, the differences between
named and unnamed demons and the dangers of dealing
with demons. They also learn the differences between
elementals and spirits, and what each is useful for.

Not all of this information is correct. Some of it turns
out to be outdated or to have been recorded, reported or
translated incorrectly, But by and large, students of the
Heptagram have a much easier time dealing with spirits
than other Dynasts do. Some students who do not go on to
become sorcerers become advisors to important politicians
and military leaders who expect to deal with spirits.


### Engineering and Chemistry


Sorcery is the subject of unshakable natural laws. Just
as Manse construction involves particular angles, repeating motifs and exact measurements, so do many other
elements of sorcery. Knowledge of such sacred geometry
and architecture is critical to successful magical workings.
A knowledgeable Dynast has a much easier time when
dealing with fellow sorcerers and Manse architects, as he
can check their designs and preparations for himself. He
might not be able to make sense of everything others do,
but at least he can catch glaring errors. This has saved more
than one Dynast from a treacherous contractor.

Chemicals, herbal preparations and drugs work the
same way. Certain substances can be combined in repeatable ways to make poisons, healing drugs, tranquilizers,
hallucinogens and fertility drugs. Many graduates of the
Heptagram possess the knowledge and skill of pharmacists,
healers and alchemists. Dynasts often prefer to see a doctor
or healer who attended the Heptagram.

How THEY’RE SEEN

Sorcerers make almost everyone at least a little nervous. After all, the powers they wield are dangerous, and
few outside of their little society understand even a fraction of those powers. They also tend to see the world and
those around them in odd and unusual ways, and this
makes people uncomfortable.

Some people resent sorcerers — they’d like to be able
to do what sorcerers can, but they lack the talent, the
patience or the ambition to learn all the ins and outs of the
arts. Some lack the connections to get into the Heptagram
or flunked out, or their families refuse to allow them to
follow the sorcerer’s way. Others fear sorcerers — the
threat of a sorcerer’s vengeance is not to be taken lightly,
and no one wants a summoning or other experiment going
wrong in their home. Still others look down on sorcerers
and see them as twisted and power-hungry, a danger to
themselves and others. Yet many Dynasts, regardless of
their personal feelings about sorcerers, rely on their abilities and would hardly make a move without them.

Some bonded circles refuse to take sorcerers as members;
others search far and wide to take one on, and in every
generation, there are some circles made up entirely of sorcerers. Sorcerers have a reputation that far exceeds the abilities
that mast of them possess. Many Great Houses provide
separate quarters for their sorcerous members, away from the
rest of the family in case of a mishap. Some families are eager
to marry their children off to sorcerers in the hopes of
harnessing some of that power. Others refuse to marry their
children off to those “monsters,” though, of course, they don’t
voice such opinions within hearing distance of the Empress or
her eldest and most magically adept child, Mnemon.

Although some people try to enhance their own
reputation by pretending that sorcerers don’t bother them,
very few actually feel that way. The actions and mistakes
that sorcerers take and make can have disastrous consequences, While Charms and Hearthstones can have great
effects, these are seen as more natural things, a part of who
the Dragon-Bloadedare. Because sorcery is entirely learned,
many see it as an unnatural activity.


### Making Friends

: chose sorcerers who wish to be seen in.a more positive
ighit usually take one of two tacks. The first:is to hide all

The second tack is tod
others see their powers in

their art. They offer to use their knowledge and abilities to
help their house, family and friends in any way they can.

Do any of these efforts pay off? On an individual scale,
it sometimes helps. A Dynast whose life was saved by her
brother the sorcerer is more likely to view him ina positive
light. A Great House whose sorcerous members go to great
lengths to be at its beck and call will appreciate them far
more than they might scorn them as dangerous freaks.

A few sorcerers get by on an excess of charm and wit,
their people skills overcoming the stigma associated with
their activities. These are the sorcerers who prove the
exception to the rule, who are invited to salons and slept
with by handsome society lads.


### Society


Society rules that apply to the rest of the Dragon-
Blooded have unspoken variations where sorcerers are
concerned. These usually work against the sorcerer. Some
Dynasts, however, in their fear of sorcery and possible
retaliation for bad treatment, treat sorcerers better than
other Dragon-Bloods, although the element of fear in such
treatment makes it just as uncomfortable as its opposite.
Thus, though opinions vary about magic, sorcerers are
rarely comfortable anywhere.

Family income is close to that of the sorcerer’s siblings.
A slightly larger income may be an attempt to bribe the
sorcerer into “behaving” or staying away from the family. A
slightly smaller income is seen as a punishment for going
into sorcery or a not-so-subtle hint that the sorcerer isn’t
wanted and should go seek his fortune elsewhere.

Marriage is also a problem for sorcerers. Many families
don’t want their children marrying sorcerers, so it is
difficult for a sorcerer to find a good match. Sorcerers often
settle for low-ranking mates.

It is rare for a sorcerer to be left out of a gala, but it
does happen, and few Dynasts will fault the host for his
decision or speak up on the sorcerer’s behalf. A sorcerer
is seen as a dampener on what might otherwise be a
joyous occasion. Sorcerers are almost never just invited
to small parties or salons.

While households are still obliged to host visiting
sorcerers, many put such guests in distant quarters. Such
households house sorcerers poorly, speak to them curtly and
serve them inad qua ly. This is technically against tradition, but-only re very secure in their station

sorcerers — perhaps the long years of study fail to appeal
to such a hedonistic house.

House Ragara is well known for inviting sorcerers to
its Gateway tournaments. As Gateway is seen as a sport of
the mind, there are a number of sorcerers who are very well
versed in its strategies. Ragara values these skills, even ifa
sorcerer practices them. Sorcerers might, however, be kept
out of some of the wilder parties held at these tournaments.
Most families in House Ragara claim not to be bothered by
sorcery and sorcerers, but a strong undercurrent of discrimination runs through the house. It’s almost never
overt, but it shows up in little ways.

Very few sorcerers are allowed into House V’neef's
parties. V’neef values the sometimes wild, sometimes mellow, but always celebratory tone of its parties. Itdoesn’t want
to risk this mood by bringing in sorcerers. In other matters,
V'neef isn't particularly prejudiced against sorcerers, either
in the family or through marriage — it can’t afford to be. It’s
too young a house to cut itself off from such a resource.

Nellens greedily courts any sorcerer it can find. The
lack of Dragon-Blooded produced by this house leaves it
with very few resources in this area. The house hopes that
by courting sorcerers for marriage who are passed over by
the other houses, it can infuse its bloodlines with more
Exalted and gain the protection of the powerful sorcerers.
Nellens tends to overestimate the abilities of sorcerers,
however, having so little experience with them. This
results in uncomfortable confrontations in the family and
some unwarranted disappointment. Nellens likes to show
off the sorcerers it courts and, thus, is perfectly willing to
include sorcerers in its parties.

Tepet maintains a cool attitude toward sorcerers,
neither overtly snubbing them, nor courting them. Family
members are not encouraged to go to the Heptagram, but
neither are they discouraged from it. Its family sorcerers are
usually invited to parties when socially appropriate, but
they aren’t sought out for the purpose.

House Mnemon encourages its children to delve into
sorcery, and thus, a high numberof Mnemon possessskill with
the art. Mnemon herself isa powerful sorceress, and her house
likes to show off its sorcerers. House Mnemon’s displays are
subtler than Nellens.’ Sorcerers are treated pretty much like
any other member of the family, except that more work is put
into finding them appropriate marriages and into placing
them where they can do the most good. Mnemon parties

families do not agree with the greater house on this issue.
Because Ledaal parties tend to be smaller affairs, whether or
not a sorcerer attends depends more on any close attachments he has to the host and less on his status as a sorcerer.

House Peleps sees sorcerers primarily as tools to be used,
not as family members or beloved siblings. Only children
who are not valued, who are seen as damaged goods in some
way, who have shamed their parents or whose parents have
brought shame upon themselves are allowed to attend the
Heptagram. Sorcerers are almost never invited to Peleps
parties, unless it’s a part of someone’s strategy.


### Societies


Because sorcerers are, in many ways, cut off from normal
Dynastic society, they tend to organize themselves into small
societies. It’s a way to feel comfortable among peers, it's a way
to feel less alone or lonely, it’s valuable networking, and it
gives them parties to attend where they don't feel unwanted.
It’s also a manifestation of all the academic differences that
pop up among those who study the natural laws of sorcery.

Many societies are broken up along lines of magical
lineage — in other words, one society might consist of a
teacher, his students and their students. Others are broken
up along lines of study — a number of sorcerers who follow
a certain viewpoint about sorcery might join together.
Often, these two are the same, as a sorcerer usually passes
a certain angle of study along to his students and theirs.
Other societies are regional, involve members of a specific
house or household or center around a sworn brotherhood
or a group of friends who use their sorcerous studies as an
excuse to spend time with one another.

Sorcerous societies come and go with great frequency.
Lineage groups and bonded circles tend to last the longest,
while those organized around a political cause form, break
up and reform all the time. Societies espousing particular
lines of study or belief usually last for at least a decade or two.
By then, either their possibilities are exhausted, or they’ve
lost their popularity, or they've gained enough ofa following
that they'll survive for quite some time to come. Regional or
house-based societies tend to last, as they offer sorcerers
benefits beyond just the sorcerous. Regional societies, however, rarely achieve much beyond information dispersal, a
little networking and some fun f parties, as they have few ways
of commanding loyalty among their members.

Societies can n be open ‘or secret ‘All of. the members

THE MNEMON Darik SOCIETY

This lineal society was brought together by Mnemon
Darik, a sorcerer of some renown. He isn’t particularly
powerful, but he did have the ear of the Empress, and he
knows a couple of unusual spells that most other sorcerers
do not. He is generous with his time in terms of mentoring
students. His detractors say that this is because he isn’t
good for much else. He has a warm, fatherly personality
however, and his students have only good things to say
about him. The society includes Darik’s four original
students and almost all of their students as well.

The society doesn’t do much in terms of causes or goals.
However, it does serve as a useful information network.
Because Darik fosters such a familial atmosphere among his
students most of them are still fond of him and are happy to
pass on interesting tidbits of knowledge. Whenever something bad happens to someone in Darik’s sorcerous lineage,
he takes on her students as his own. Members of this group
find it relatively easy to get help from the others during an
emergency or when searching for a job.

Darik has a grudge against the Heptagram and prefers to
teach those sorcerers who, for whatever reason, were unable
to attend or graduate from the school. He himself washed out
of the Heptagram and went on to sorcerous greatness.


### The Animists


The Animists are a group of sorcerers who believe
that, since sorcery follows natural laws, the best place to
learn about sorcery is in nature. They prefer to learn
through observation of nature and studying with spirits.
The Animists are largely ignored by other sorcerous societies and sometimes regarded as cranks. Most Animists are
relatively unskilled, since they concentrate on learning
things from the ground up, making the same mistakes that
students of skilled sorcerers learn to avoid.

Those Animists who do become powerful sorcerers are
impressive indeed, usually through combining the understanding they have gained through animism with some
practical schooling. Animism seems to lend an intuitive feel
for the laws of sorcery, but this needs to be paired with more
traditional schooling to be of much use. Unfortunately for

### Checks and Balances


The Empress knew that sorcerers could be a danger
to her power. The ability to research and perform powerful and previously unheard-of spells was a threat. Because
of this, she used several means to keep track of or control
over the sorcerers within the Realm. The first is the
Watchers, mentioned above, who investigate sorcerers
for diabolism. But they are hardly the Empress’ only hold
over the Realm’s sorcerers.


### The White Registry

The Empress required every Dragon-Blooded sorcerer

who. knew even a single spell to register as a sorcerer with’

There are some Dragon-Blooded who have used this

### Obligations


The law of the Realm is that each sorcerer, upon
graduation from the Heptagram or entry into the White
Registry, owes the Realm a single Obligation. This means
that the Empress or the Deliberative may, at any time, call
upon a Dynastic sorcerer to perform a single task, great or
small, for the Realm, without any promised reward or
recognition. The sorcerer must then never speak of the
form his Obligation took to anyone.

This Obligation usually took one of several forms.
The Empress sometimes used it to take care of some matter
that was bothering her or to discharge a debt she owed. She
either tailored the Obligation to the skills of the sorcerer
or, if she took interest in the sorcerer, used it to push him
to greater feats of skill.

More often, however, she used the Obligation to
make a point to the sorcerer. If she heard that the sorcerer
had no respect for her position, she would choose a task
that would demonstrate her power, her largess, her intelligence or anything else that would make him rethink his
position. If the sorcerer was someone she wished to recruit
for the Watchers, she might choose a task that would show
him the horrors of diabolism. If she needed someone to
take a position in a certain prefecture, she might choose a
task that showed the sorcerer he was needed there.

The Realm is a mighty empire, the greatest and most
powerful state in recorded memory. But empires are not
faceless monoliths, norare they collectives of hive-minded
drones. Like any empire, the Realm is made up of individuals, each with his own complex hopes, goals, dreams and
desires. You are about to become one of those individuals.
Not just any individual, though; you are one of the Terrestrial Exalted — the Dragon-Blooded. By virtue of blood
and birth, you are one of the Realm’s ruling elite. By virtue
of spiritual enlightenment, you are literally a bodhissatva
among men, an enlightened saint destined to rule because
of your divine might and spiritual advancement.

Thischapter provides the rules needed tocreate Dragon-
Blooded characters. The process for creating one of the
Terrestrial Exalted follows the same guidelines presented for
Solars in Exalted. However, there are areas in which the
Terrestrial: Exalted differ from their fallen Solar cousins.
ing material details these differences and is


### The Storyteller


During character creation, it is important for
the Storyteller to work with her players, and vice
versa. Ultimately, gaming is a team effort, and the
closer everyone works together, the more fun
everyone will have. This is especially true for the
Dragon-Blooded, who were created by the gods to
be more powerful as a group than they are as
individuals. Storytellers should discuss with their
players the type of series they want to run, and
players should work to create character concepts
that fir that type of series. Players should also work
at making their characters fully developed personalities, with histories and goals. The more raw
material a Storyteller has to work with, the more
story hooks she can provide for that character.

<!--PAGE 147-->
superior to mortal menand, as such, are suited to rule over the
unExalred. But while they stand at the top of the Perfected
Hierarchy, expectationsdemand that Dragon-Blooded maintain their positions within certain portions of society.

® The Dynasty: At the pinnacle of the social strata are
the 10 Great Houses, each descending from the Scarlet
Empress. Also included is House Nellens, whose members
descend from one of the Empress’s consorts. At least 90
percent of all Terrestrial Exalted are born among the families of the Dynasty, and they are the elite of the Realm. The
Dynasty is a divine aristocracy whose members are born and
raised knowing that one day they will assume their rightful
place as leaders within the greatest empire in all Creation.

® The Order of the Immaculate Dragons: Keepers of
the Realm’s state religion, the Order is home to more
Dragon-Blooded than any group save the Dynasty itself. It
is both a place of refuge for those members of the Dynasty
seeking escape from the pressures of family politics and a
path of power for those lowborn Dragon-Blooded seeking
to advance their place within the Perfected Hierarchy.

® The Thousand Scales: The bureaucratic web that
governs the Realm, the ranks of the Thousand Scales are
largely filled with unExalted. Those Dragon-Blooded among
the Imperial Service are primarily members of the legions,
agents of the All-Seeing Eye, magistrates or high-ranking
members of the ministries. Dynastic and patrician houses
place Dragon-Blooded members within the Thousand Scales,
and many born outside the Dynasty find the legions and the
magistrates an easy path to power and influence.

® Outcastes: Those Dragon-Blooded who turn
their backs on the Realm or who are born outside of the
Realm and show no desire to take their proper place
within the Perfected Hierarchy are considered vile
deviants, little better than the Anathema. Any Dragon-
Blooded who is not part of a Dynasty household or a
member of the Order of Immaculate Dragons or the
Thousand Scales is considered outcaste.

Order, though some are well educated enough to,
enter the Thousand Scales. Those born of ¢ citizen,
peasant or disenfranchised stock who are Exalted;
by pure:chance of heredity almost invariably join :
the Inimaculates, though some few join the le-:
gions. The rest become outcastes. (°°, i

choose their spouses when they choose to align with one
house or another. Some do so out of loyalty or love; most
do so out of political expediency. Those non-Dynasts
among the Immaculate Order or the Thousand Scales are
under no compulsion to join one of the Great Houses, but
little stands in the way of those who wish to do so.

For full details on the Great Houses, see Chapter Two:
The Dragon-Blooded.


### Elemental Aspect


Unlike the debased Anathema, whose dark patrons
divide their lackeys into castes, the virtuous Dragon-Blooded
-are divided into five elemental aspects. These aspects corre-
“spond to. the Five Elemental Dragons (Air, Earth, Fire,

that they might.de troy the Anathema and: lead others

### Anima Effects


Just as the Anathema are able to channel their corrupt
power through their anima banners tocreate horrific effects,
a Dragon-Blooded can attune her anima to her aspected
element to generate miraculous effects. In addition to the
abilities unique to their aspects, all Dragon-Blooded can
spend a single mote of Essence to:

® Cause the skin tint natural to her aspect to deepen
and become slightly luminescent for a scene (clearly
marking the character as one of the Chosen of the
Elemental Dragons).

® Cause her anima to glow brightly enough read by
for a scene.

® Know her exact location in relation to her aspect’s
corresponding elemental pole (enablinga Dragon-Blooded
character to subtract 1 from the difficulty of a roll to
navigate or orient herself).


### Nature


Nature functions identically for Terrestrial Exalted as
it does for Anathema. A number of Dragon-Blooded
posses N itures that are somewhat common among their

Or House & ASPECT

Ten of the 11 Great Houses of the Imperial
Dynasty are oriented toward onc of the five holy
elements, and these elemental affinities have
some connection to each house’s temperament
and place within the Realm. Each house affinity
represents the elemental aspect of the house’s
founder, and many of the house elders share this
affinity. However, not all — or even most — of
a house’s newly Exalted Dragon-Bloods match
their house’s elemental affinity. Centuries of
arranged marriages, romantic dalliances and adoptions have ensured that each house includes a
sizable number of Dragon-Blooded representing
each element. From time to time, house elders
and the leaders of the Immaculate Order debate
just what, if anything, this portends.

And what does this mean for your character?
Simply that you can play a character of any
elemental aspect from any house you wish. Houses
and aspects are meant to provide character options, not limitations.

STEP Two: CHOOSING

### Attributes


As with all characters, Dragon-Blooded begin with
each Attribute at one dot. Being spiritually advanced
beings, Dragon-Blooded have much more potential than
mundane mortals. However, they are somewhat weaker
than the accursed Anathema, who draw their unnatural
strength from the forces of corruption.

A Dragon-Bloaded character has seven (7) dots to
divide among her primary Attributes, six (6) dots to
divide among her secondary Attributes and four (4) dots
to divide among her tertiary Attributes. Characters are


### Paragons of Virtue


One Nature deserves special attention. For
many: Dragon-Blooded, especially those who
are members of the Order of the Immaculate
Dragons, the Paragon archetype reflects a striving to embody the ideals espoused by the
Immaculate Dragon the character wishes to
emulate. The archetype functions the same as it
would for any other character. The Realm’s
culture simply has a very specific idea about
what the heroic ideal is. See page 51 of the
Exalted main rulebook for a description of the
Five Dragons and their ideals.

<!--PAGE 149-->
not limited in how many dots they may assign to each
Attribute. Attributes may not be raised above five dots.


### Step Three: Choosing Abilities


Abilities begin with a rating of zero and are divided
according to which element each skill is attuned to.

¢ Air Abilities are skills related to subtlety in both
thought and motion. They are Linguistics, Lore, Occult,
Stealth and Thrown.

® Earth Abilities are skills that relate io solidness,
stability and creation. They are Awareness, Craft, Endurance, Martial Arts and Resistance.

® Fire Abilities are skills devoted to energy and
passion. They are Athletics, Dodge, Melee, Presence and
Socialize.

© Water Abilities are skills that involve mystery and
fluidity of both mind and body. They are Brawl, Bureaucracy, Investigation, Larceny and Sail.

® Wood Abilities are those skills devoted to life and
nature. They are Archery, Medicine, Performance, Ride
and Survival.

Every Dragon-Blooded has a natural affinity with her
element’s Abilities. This affinity is represented by the
easier time a character has in raising her Aspect Abilities
through both bonus and experience points. See the table
on page 153 for the bonus point costs.

Dragon-Blooded also have an affinity with certain
Abilities outside of their Aspect Abilities. A character
selects three (3) Abilities to be her Favored Abilities. These
abilities receive the same bonus and experience point
discounts as her Aspect Abilities. You may not “double up”
and increase your discount by choosing Aspect Abilities as
Favored Abilities.

Young Dynasts are pressured throughout their young
lives to learn and are phenomenally skilled compared to
the average person. You have 13 points to spend on a
Dragon-Blood’s Aspect and Favored Abilities and an
additional 22 points to spend on any Abilities you wish.
Characters do not have to possess any dots in their Aspect
Abilities. The potential co easily learn an Ability does not
necessarily’ mean a character has devoted any time toward
rninigit. However, a character must possess.at least one

is a “Favored” Ability is that you have devo
toward learning it. =

All Dynasts must have an absolute minimum of Archery ®, Brawl or Martial Arts ©, Melee ®, Performance
¢, Presence ®, Ride ®, Lore ©® and Socialize @®, to
represent the basic training in martial matters and social
skills that forms the underpinnings of their culture. Most
will have all these Abilities at © ® or more, and those who
do not will generally be regarded by their peers as deficient
in critical life skills. Characters raised outside the Dynasty
are not required to meet these minimums.

STEP Four: ADVANTAGES

Because of the immense opportunities and resources
made available to the Dragon-Blooded of the Realm,
players have 12 dots to spend on Backgrounds. In addition,
the complex culture of the Dynasty gives Dragon-Blooded
characters access to a wider selection of Backgrounds from
which to select. See page 156 for full details on these new
and altered Backgrounds.

The spiritually advanced nature of the Dragon-Blooded
gives them access to powers above those of mortals. However, some Dragon-Blooded have undergone the spiritual
training needed to refine their souls even further and allow
them to master the sacred war arts of the Elemental Dragons.

### Step Five: Finishing Touches


Calculate a Dragot

<!--PAGE 151-->
Personal Essence pool is calculated by adding your
Essence and Willpower ratings (Essence + Willpower),
plus any additional points granted by the Breeding Background (see page 158 of the Traits chapter).

Peripheral Essence pool is calculated by multiplying
your Essence Trait by four and then adding the sum of your
Willpower and your two highest Virtues [(Essence x 4) +
Willpower + (thesum of your two highest Virtues)], plus any
additional points granted by the Breeding Background.

Dragon-Blooded characters posses the normal mortal
seven health levels — a -0 health level, two.-1 health
levels, two -2 health levels, a -4 health level and an
Incapacitated health level — plus any additional health
levels gained from the Ox-Body Technique.

Dragon-Blooded characters have 15 bonus points that
may be spent at any time during character creation. See the
chart on page 153 for the point cost of raising each Trait.

‘THE SPARK OF LIFE

Now come the final touches. Answer the same questions for your Dragon-Blooded character that you would for
any other Exalt: What does she look like? What are her
habits and quirks? Who is she close to? Who are her enemies
and rivals? What was her childhood like? When did she first
Exalt? What motivates her to do the things she does?

In addition, there are other questions to answer.

® Family Life: How does her immediate family regard
the character? This means both her parents and also the
household she’s living with. What does the rest of her
house think of her, ifanything? Has she done anything to
attract the house elders’ attention (for good or ill)? If so,
what was it? Is anyone else in her immediate family one of
the Dragon-Blooded, or is she the first in recent generations? Have any of her unExalted relatives attempted to
attach their fortunes to hers?

® Marriage: Most Realm marriages are arranged affairs, often planned while the couple is still very young. Is

### Artifact


As lords of the Realm, the Dragon-Blooded have
inherited a sizable portion of the remains of the First Age’s
infrastructure, as well as almost the entirety of its arsenal of
jade artifacts and wonders. The result of this is that Dragon-
Blooded characters have a much easier time acquiring
artifacts than the fallen Anathema (or anyone else, for that
matter). The Artifact Background grants Dragon-Blooded
twice the number of dots worth of artifacts as normal.

For more on Artifacts, see Chapter Nine: Wonders and
Equipment in Exalted and also The Book of Three Circles.

Storyteller’s choice.

level 2 artifact, a pair of level 2
artifacts anda level 1 artifact, etc.).

character may also have access to
the Realm’s arsenal of First Age
war machines (not that this mean’s
he'll actually own one, but he may
be able to get permission to use one).

all known Manses located on the Blessed Isle. Because

hold these Manses, it’s exceedingly difficult for a

personal property. Most households and estates are
built around Manses, and being able to purchase the
rights to one is essential in starting a household or line.
Rather than representing a single Manse that the
character has claim on, the Manse rating of a Dragon-
Blooded character indicates generally how many
Manses she has been attuned to. Characters who carry
the Hearthstone from a Manse probably don’t actually
own it, instead holding it on extended loan. In case of
family emergency, the stone may be recalled. This is
especially true with powerful Hearthstones. Level 1
and 2 Manses are very common in the Realm and are
used as weekend and vacation manors, hunting lodges,
strongholds to defend small estates and the like. It’s
unlikely anyone is going to urgently send for her
Hearthstone unless the Realm is at war.

### Command


Be it through personal financing, family connections or actual military skill, the character has earned
the right to command soldiers. The troops may be
members of the legions, or they may be part of his
house’s private army. They are under the character's
direct command, and unlike military forces gained from
the Backing Background, they don't have anything to
do other than take the character's orders. These soldiers
do not accompany the character everywhere he goes
(that’s the job of Henchmen), but they will typically
remain close by and can be brought on risky endeavors.
If the character loses his soldiers, they will generally be
teplaced, but if they die due to incompetence, he'll
probably be removed from command instead.

The loyalty of these soldiers depends upon their
treatment. An officer who wastes his men’s lives or
abuses their trust will find those he commands less
than enthusiastic about following his orders or coming
to his aid in times of trouble. Keep in mind that
characters whose soldiers occupy government buildings or use their troops to depose the local prefect will
generally be considered to be participating in an armed
insurrection and may very well get the character executed, disowned or both.

Troops that come from this Background use the Elite
Troops template found on page 278 of Exalted. They are
considered to be extras.

X None; you either haven’t earned
the right to lead troops or you have
no interest in doing so.

and exceeding that amount taxes the character’s mind
and body beyond its limits.

### Henchmen

Henchmen are created using the rules for mortal

(such as Martial Arts for Immaculate acolytes or Socialize for skilled socialites). Henchmen are loyal to the

they realize they have been deliberately betrayed. Characters who want legions of fanatical assassins should

### Reputation


The society of the Dragon-Blooded, especially
among the Dynasty, is tightly knit, even incestuous,
with news and gossip traveling at lightning speed. The
result of this is that a character’s notable exploits — at
least those she doesn’t take pains to conceal — will
soon become well known by both her peers and the
Dynasty or even the Realm as a whole. This reputation
can be positive or negative (or even both at once), and
it will affect the way others view your character. In
situations the Storyteller deems it appropriate, a character may add his Reputation rating to his dice pool
when making Social rolls. In situations where the
reputation is a liabiliry, the character subtracts an
equal number of dice.

Itis important for the player and the Storyteller to
work together to create as much detail as possible for
the character's reputation,.as these details will influence how and when'the Background comes into play.

merchant in the city.

across the Realm.

Your legend has preceded you even
to the far corners of the Threshold.


### Other Rules


There are a number of other, minor rules regarding
the Dragon-Blooded that are presented here. Where not
specifically contradicted by these rules, Dragon-Blooded
are otherwise treated exactly as any other sort of Exalted.
This includes but is not limited to the rate at which they
heal, their resistance to poison and disease, their ability to
suppress bleeding, the speed at which they regain Essence,
the visibility and size of their anima banner and the rate at
which it dissipates and all the other characteristics that
distinguish Exalted from mere mortals.

ANIMA Flux

Dragon-Blooded have very powerful elemental animas.
While those of the other Exalted can be so intense as to be
unsettling or mildly discomfiting, those of the Terrestrial
Exalted are so raw and powerful as to actually inflict
damage. This is the reason the Dragon-Blooded donot ride
horses to war. The damage varies, and may seem to come
from biting winds, scratching thorns or burning flame, but
the source is the same —~ the raw elemental power of the
character's Essence.

If che Dragon-Blooded’s anima is at the 8-10 motes
level, then it will do one die of lethal damage for every
minute of contact. Characters that can soak lethal
damage with their Stamina are hardy enough to resist
this damage with their supernatural constitutions, but
roll:one die‘for every minute of contact with the anima

“mortal creature.

of Fire who have activated their anima power add this to
the anima banner, while other Exalts simply do one per
tum. This damage is soaked normally, but even Exalted
and other magical beings can be injured by the display.

The negative effects of the Dragon-Blooded aura do
not apply to the Dragon-Blooded’s gear, but they radiate
from the Exalr a number of feet equal to the character’s
permanent Essence. It can and will damage beings within
its radius and will also have detrimental effects on the
scenery. This is especially dangerous for Aspects of Fire,
who can accidentally burn down forests and cities, but all
Dragon-Blooded animas will destroy cloth and soft wood
within a minute. It is for this reason that the Terrestrials
favor sturdy stone construction in places where their
anima banners may flare.


### Sworn Brothers’ Oath

This Terrestrial Circle sorcery spell binds the Essence of two or more Exalted together and is the one used
in the creation of sworn brotherhoods. The ritual takes
an hour to perform upon two Exalted, and one additional
hour for each Exalt beyond that. The sorcerer does not
have to be one of the Exalted bound by the spell. Once
the spell is cast, the sorcerer’s player makes an Intelligence + Occult roll. The number of successes scored on
this roll indicates the level of the sworn brotherhood.
The bound Exalted are now connected on a spiritual
level, resulting in several effects.

¢ Loyalty: In order to take an action she knows will
harm — mentally, emotionally or physically — her
sworn brother, a character's player must make a Conviction roll (which can be an extended roil) and score a
number of successes equal to the level of the sworn
brotherhood. So, if the sorcerer gained 6 successes while
casting the spell, the player must score 6 successes to
break her character’s oath of brotherhood.

* Devotion: When a character undertakes an action
with the deliberate purpose of aiding her blood-brother,
her player may add additional dice to her dice pool for that

The Immaculate Dragons: Air-aspected Dragon-
Blooded who follow the way of Mela are often staunch
perfectionists, endlessly refining their elaborate plans
until they reach perfection. They are often among the
Realm's premiere military strategists, scholars and sorcerers. Followers of Sextes Jylis direct their dreams
toward the improvement of the ‘Realm, and these
Chosen are often willing to consider the impact their
schemes may have on others. Emulators of Pasiap focus
their thoughts toward spiritual enlightenment, both
for themselves and others. Air Exalted seeking the
path of Hesiesh are among the most elitist of their
kind, reserving their thoughts for matters of great
importance. Those who emulate Daana’d push
their minds to the limit, striving to master the most
esoteric or even dangerous forms of knowledge.

Aspect Markings: Aspects of Air are typically thin
and lithe. Their skin has a natural bluish or whitish tint.
Blue tones are most pronounced among those with high
Breeding. As their Essence increases, Air-aspected Dragon-
Blooded begin to be surrounded by a continuous gusting
breeze, which increases in intensity when they are angry or
agitated. This wind may be stilled through concentration
but returns if the character’s mind becomes preoccupied
(such as by combat, an argument, deep focus on a manuscript or sex). Elders are often characterized by their
ice-cold skin and by the scent of fresh breezes or thunder.

Aspect Anima: When an Air-aspected Dragon-
Blooded spends Peripheral Essence, his anima radiates
outward into a shimmering aura of pale blue and white,
swirling in patterns that remind one of rolling clouds or
gusting winds. Truly grand displays include phantasmal
whirlwinds, lightning, air dragons or swarms of snowflakes,

while the sounds of roaring thunder or screaming wind
echo around the character.

An Aspect of Air dan spend 5 motes to attune his
anima to the nearby winds, allowing them to bear him
aloft. For the rest of the scene, he may triple his leaping
distance and takes no damage from falls.

Aspect Abilities: Air Exalted are creatures of thought
and dream, excelling in areas of scholarship and subtle
motion. They have a natural affinity with the Abilities of
Linguistics, Lore, Occult, Stealth and Thrown.

Common Natures: Architect, Bureaucrat, Conniver,
Explorer, Gallant, Judge, Leader, Paragon, Rebel, Savant,
Thriliseeker, Visionary

The Great Curse: Aspects of Air whose curse lies in
their Compassion often hold idealistic views of how the
world should work and react poorly when confronted with
how cruel society can be. Those whose curse derives from
Conviction often become so fixated upon their dreams of
the future that, like blizzards, they become deaf to the cries
of the present, enabling them to reach great heights of
cruelty in pursuit of their schemes. Temperate Air Exalted
are like calm breezes, tending to be moral idealists. When
they find themselves or others unable to live up to their
own lofty dreams of purity, they frequently vent their
frustrations with all the fury of a lightning storm. Those
who suffer curses of Valor often take excessive pride in
their own mental facilities and will react with tornado-like
violence to having their skills questioned or trust in their
own plans and abilities to the point of recklessness.

Associations: The color blue, the Maiden of Serenity, the season of Air and the northern direction

Sobriquets: Children of Mela, Tempestuous Knives,
Azure Dragons

Concepts: Scholar of the forbidden, idealistic monk,
daring sorcerer, political puppetmaster, languid dreamer,
philosopher-general, earnest bureaucrat

Children of Pasiap intuitively understand the importance of enduring

chaos. Things become ritual and tradition because they are important —

An Earth-aspected Dragon-

because she believes these rites are
guaranteed to produce stable relationships.Shefollowsthe teachings

others, it is like solid granite, unmoving

and the centuries.
A Child of Pasiap places importance on those things that, like

more important
than fleeting dalliaie ances. Long-term

<!--PAGE 167-->
providing a stable foundation for Dynastic society. Staunch
supporters of tradition, Aspects of Earth believe that which
once allowed the Realm to prosper will help it grow strong
again and stubbornly defend those institutions in which they
believe. That which they consider important remains worthiest of protection, they reason, and all else deserves whatever
fate befalls it. Ifsomething new can endure on its own, then it
may be worthy of support. If not, then it was of little consequence to begin with.

Aspects of Earth perform similar duties within swom brothethoods. Their iron wills provide a pillar of strength from which
the brotherhood may draw support, and their firmness in their
convictions ensures that the brotherhood carries out any action
itcommits to. The tradition of the bonded circle is one with long
and distinguished history. An Earth-aspected Dragon-Blooded
will be very cautious about committing to such a group, but once
swom, her dedication to the brotherhood is unshakable.

The Immaculate Dragons: Earth-aspected who follow
Pasiap are among the Realm’s greatest builders, not just of
grand cathedrals that demonstrate the enduring glory of
spiritual enlightenment, but also of political coalitions, economic empires and stalwart armies. Those who walk the path
of Hesiesh are among the Realm’s staunchest upholders of
tradition, often ignoring or even mistrusting anything contemporary or untested. Earth Exalted who follow Daana’d
attempt to push their own endurance to its limits, using
exhaustive martial arts rituals, strenuous asceticism and challengingsorceriestorefineand purifying their Essence. Emulators
of Mela demand nothing less than perfection in themselves as
they maintain the traditions of the past. They seck excellence
in all aspects of Realm society, quoting from the Immaculate
Texts and ancient treatises on the arts of war, courtship and
politics with equal ease. Those who follow Sextes Jylis often
travel the Realm, teaching the importance of adhering to
tradition, while relentlessly hunting down heretics.

Aspect Markings: Aspects of Earth are frequently
compact orstocky. Their skin hasa brownish or gray tint that
is even darker among those of good breeding. As Earthaspected Dragon-Blooded age, their skin begins to harden,
taking on a stone-like texture that ranges from smooth
marble to rough sandstone. Elders often smell of freshly
tumed soil, moist clay or dry sand.

Aspect Anima: When an Earth-aspected Dragon-
Blooded spends Peripheral Essence, her anima erupts into a
mantle of white and yellow light, shifting like rolling sand or
glimmering like a perfect diamond. Awe-inspiring uses of
Essence produce phantasmal images of badgers, bulls, stone
pillars, the Imperial Mountain or earth dragons, as the character issurrounded by a thunderous roar suchas that produced by
an avalanche, volcano or earthquake.

An Aspect of Earth can spend 5 motes to attune her
anima to the nearby stone, taking on the durability of the
earth itself. For the rest of the scene, she can soak lethal
damage with her entire Stamina.

Aspect Abilities: Aspects of Earth are living embodiments of stability, witha keen insight into the importance of
tradition, ritual and that which endures. They excel at
shaping things that last, be it buildings and tools or their own
bodies and souls. Earth-aspected Dragon-Blooded possess a
natural affinity with the Abilities of Awareness, Crafts,
Endurance, Martial Arts and Resistance.

Common Natures: Architect, Bravo, Bureaucrat,
Caregiver, Critic, Follower, Judge, Leader, Martyr, Paragon,
Survivor, Traditionalist, Visionary

The Great Curse: An Earth-aspected Dragon-Blooded
whose flaw involves Compassion will often orient her empathy toward those institutions she cherishes and protects,
reacting with earthshaking anger or despair when people who
are part of that institution are jeopardized. One who manifests
a Conviction flaw is often willing to go to extremes of cruelty
and sadism in order to uphold traditions and ideals — and to
punish those who violate them. A Temperate Earth Exalted
frequently seeks to focus her existence on only important
matters and seeks to destroy those who cannot match herown
fanatical level of devotion. If the flaw is one of Valor, she likely
reacts with volcanic anger when cherished traditions are
challenged or else becomes willing to engage in extremely
dangerous undertakings with the belief that tried and true
methods will undoubtedly see her through to success.

Associations: The color white, the Maiden of Battle,
the season of Earth and the central direction

Sobriquets: Children of Pasiap, Stone Fists, lvory Dragons

_: Concepts: Masterarchitect, military strategist, wandering monk, dedicated magistrate, Realm historian

Reeds

Fire burns. This is the very nature of its being —
when an object ceases burning, the fire is not sleeping,
but dead. And so it is with the Exalted of Fire. Their
hearts burn with a passion few others will ever
match. To the Children of Hesiesh, passion is everything, and one who no longer feels or believes is no
longer alive at all.

A Fire-aspected Dragon-Blood is passion incarnate. When she loves, she loves with all her
heart. When she believes, she believes with unsurpassed zealotry. When she hates, she hates
with the burning intensity of the Southernmost flames. For some, passion burns like a
slow, smoldering bed of coals, quiet and
almost unnoticeable until one gets close
enough. The hearts of others burn with the
raging power of an inferno, visible for all to see
and blasting anything that stands before it.

No action is ever meaningless to a
Child -of Hesiesh. The Aspects of Fire
enact religious rituals because they truly
believe in their faith. Conflicts — be
they martial, political or social — are
undertaken because they truly believe in

hearts of others. Aspects of Fire challenge complacency, attacking hallowed
traditions to remind people why they
follow those traditions in the first
place and playing devil’s advocate so
that ideas and beliefs are reexamined and
perfected. That which cannot withstand or be strengthened by the
Children of Hesiesh’s scrutiny is simply burned away to make room for
something more meaningful.

Aspectsof Fire serve similar roles

) anything less.

us within bonded circles, reminding
A | comrades of their purpose and

Fire-aspected Dragon-Blooded’s
loyalty to her circle is unquestionable; her passion will not allow for

a yo Aspects of Fire who follow
~~. “Hesiesh work to reserve their pas-
O sién for those things that are most

<!--PAGE 169-->
focus their passion upon the Realm itself, working to
strengthen the empire and those who dwell within ir.
Finally, those who emulate Pasiap direct their passions
toward spiritual enlightenment, frequently sharing their
passion with others as priests and instructors.

Aspect Markings: Aspects of Fire are marked by a
obvious reddish tint to their skin. This tint is even more
noticeable among those with high Breeding and becomes
darker as the Dragon-Blooded ages — well-bred elders are
often a deep and fiery crimson. It is not uncommon for
older Dragon-Blooded of this aspect to emit puffs of smoke
from their mouths as they speak or from their noses as they
breath. Some elders even radiate heat from their skin,
making them extremely warm to the touch.

Aspect Anima: When a Fire-aspected Dragon-
Blooded spends Peripheral Essence, her anima ignites into
alambent aura of reds, oranges or yellows, shimmering and
blazing like a bonfire. High Essence expenditures result in
spectacular eruptions and flickering images of tigers,
firebirds and fire dragons and the crackling roar of a forest
fire or the dull scream of a stoked blast furnace.

A Fire-aspected Dragon-Blooded can spend 5
motes to ignite her anima. For the rest of the scene, she
is immune to fire damage, and anyone striking her
without a weapon or wrestling with her takes a number
of dice of lethal damage every turn equal to the Chosen’s
Essence. If the character succeeds with an unarmed
attack or wrestles with a character, her opponent takes
a like amount of damage. This damage is applied
separately from any damage the character does with an
attack, and armor applies co it.

Aspect Abilities: As living embodiments of passion,
Fire-aspected Dragon-Blooded possess an overabundance
of personal energy, both physical and emotional. They
excel at physical activity and social interaction, giving
them a natural affinity with the Abilities of Athletics,
Dodge, Melee, Presence and Socialize.

Common Natures: Bravo, Caretaker, Critic, Explorer, Gallant, Hedonist, Jester, Leader, Martyr, Paragon,
Rebel, Survivor, Thrillseeker, Visionary

The Great Curse: A Compassionate Aspect of Fire
is often driven by her love for those under her care or
simply a deep passion for the concepts of justice and
righteousness. When that which she cares deeply about
is harmed or threatened, her passion overwhelms her and
drives her to despair, self-sacrifice or righteous fury. If her
flaw derives from her Conviction, her burning devotion
to the cause she fights for makes it possible for her to
inflict great suffering in the name of her passion. Although Temperate Fire Exalted might seem like a
contradiction, they are quite common. Such a character
will likely orient her passions toward self-perfection and
enlightenment and direct scorching hatred toward those
that cannot live up to her standards, herself included.
Valor flaws often drive a Fire-aspected Dragon-Blooded
to become a burning engine of destruction, as her passionate hatred for her foes overwhelms her.

Associations: The color red, the Maiden of Journeys,
the season of Fire and the southern direction

Sobriquets: Children of Hesiesh, Burning Swords,
Crimson Dragons

Concepts: Righteous crusader, contrary social critic,
legion commander, irrepressible socialite, devoted scholar

ita petty personal rivalry ora war that engulfs all Creation,
is an opportunity for the Realm to grow and mature. The

Aspects of Water perform similar functions within
their sworn brotherhoods, tailoring their actions to compliment the strengths and counter the weaknesses of their
sworn brothers. A Water-aspected Dragon-Blooded nurtures his brotherhood, helping the members adapt and
grow stronger in the face of adversity.

The Immaculate Dragons: Water-aspected Dragon-
Blooded who emulate Daana’d are the most devoted
adherents to the ideals of self-sufficiency and growth
through adversity. They challenge themselves to excel
in all arenas — martial, social and spiritual. Those who
follow Mela strive toward adaptability and flexibility,
especially in the arts of war. They are proficient masters
of unconventional tactics, including assassination, guerilla warfare and terrorism. Aspects of Water who follow
the ways of Sextes Jylis nurture those in need, offering
unique solutions to their troubles. They also prove
among the most adept at hunting down and eliminating
heretics. Emulators of Pasiap’s work focus their fluid
minds on the goal of enlightenment, often becoming
skilled theologians and religious instructors. Followers
of Hesiesh are staunch traditionalists and often reserve
their mercurial talents to nurture and protect that
which they hold dear.

Aspect Markings: Aspects of Water move with an
almost hypnotic grace, and their skin has a slight bluegreen tint. This aqua tone is most noticeable in members
with high Breeding, and the coloration only deepens with
age. Well-bred elders are often deep green-black or ebony
in color. They smell of fresh water, either of running
streams or the open sea.

. Aspect Anima: When a Water-aspected Dragon-
Blood spends Peripheral Essence, his anima bursts forth as
a bright blue-green or green-black glow, rippling like a
pool of water or rolling like the ocean waves. Truly
impressive displays include phantasmal whirlpools, waves,
waterspouts and water dragons accompanied by the roar of
crashing waves or the alien silence of the ocean depths.

An Aspect of Water can spend 5 motes to attune his
anima with the surrounding water. For the rest of the day,
he can breathe underwater and operate as freely as ifhe was
in air, including firing bows and the like.

Aspect Abilities: Their fluid minds grant Water
Exalted an intuitive shrewdness. They excel in navigating
their way through situations of unfathomable complexity
and at improvising their way around or through obstacles.
Aspects of Water possess a natural affinity with the Abilities of Brawl, Bureaucracy, Investigation, Larceny and
Sail.

Common Natures: Caregiver, Conniver, Critic, Explorer, Follower, Jester, Judge, Leader, Paragon, Rebel,
Savant, Survivor, Visionary

The Great Curse: A Water-aspected Dragon-Blood
whose curse lies in his Compassion is typically one who
focuses on nurturing others. He’ll go to extreme lengths to
protect that which he nurtures, sometimes drowning it in
the process. One whose curse comes from Conviction is
like a great flood, willing to wash away anything that
stands before its efforts. Temperate Aspects of Water often
become like deep, still pools, but react poorly if that
tranquility is disturbed. Those whose curse derives from
their Valor often become like tidal waves, believing there
is no problem they cannot batter through or wash away.

Associations: The color black, the Maiden of Secrets, the season of Water and the western direction

Sobriquets: Children of Daana’d, Drowning Hands,
Ebon Dragons

Concepts: Ruthless magistrate, agent of the All-
Seeing Eye, political fixer, diplomat to the Threshold,
dilettante artist

Wood-aspected Dragon-Blooded tend to the tasks that help
the empire grow strong, nurturing those people or places that
need it. The Aspects of Wood support the generals, politicians and priests they feel will help the Realm grow in the
pattern they finds most pleasing and weed out those who
might corrupt their design.

An Aspect of Wood takes on similar roles in bonded
circles. She works to shape the circle into a thing of
beauty, nurturing those of her circlemates’ traits she finds
pleasing and pruning those that might threaten the
healthy growth of her circle. ;

The Immaculate Dragons: Those Wood-aspected
Dragon-Bloods who follow Sextes Jylis are among the
greatest nurturers within the Realm, famous as healers and
protectors. Emulators of Pasiap work to guide their charges
toward deeper spiritual enlightenment and often become
relentless pruners of heresy. Followers of Hesiesh are
conservative when choosing sensations to experience,
often attempting to extend each event as long as possible.
Aspects of Wood who walk Daana’d’s path often seek out
extreme experiences, endeavoring to spiritually test both
themselves and others. Those who follow the way of Mela
strive to become perfect poison flowers, beautiful in appearance and deadly in the arts of war.

Aspect Markings: Aspects of Wood are marked with
aslight greenish tint to their skin. Among those with high
Breeding, this tint becomes almost emerald in color. As
they age, Wood-aspected Dragon-Blooded often grow a
light layer of bark on their skin, typically along the back
and shoulders. Well-bred elders sometimes sprout flowers
in their hair. Many produce a natural musk reminiscent of
flowers, pine, fresh fruit or other forms of plant life.

Aspect Anima: When a Wood-aspected Dragon-
Blooded spends Peripheral Essence, her anima blossoms into
a bright green glow, waving like a field of grass or the leaves
of atree. Large expenditures of Essence result in phantasmal
representations of trees, tangles of thors, giant flowers or
wood dragons. The sound produced is like that of a strong
wind moaning through a dense forest of towering trees. _

_A Wood-aspected Dragon-Blooded may spend 5 motes
toattune her anima to the local flora, becoming as flexible
as a sapling in the wind. For the rest of the scene, she may
add her Essence in dice to a single Dodge or Athletics
action once per turn. This power is reflexive, not supplementary, and so, the character can either add to a dice
action or gain a free Dodge or Athletics action with a dice
pool equal to her Essence.

Aspect Abilities: Aspects of Wood are intimately
tied to the cycle of life. In addition to their desire to live life
to it’s fullest, they have a natural connection with living
things — both animal and plant — and those objects
crafted from them. They possess an affinity for the Abilities of Archery, Medicine, Performance, Ride and Survival.

Common Natures: Architect, Caregiver, Conniver,
Critic, Explorer, Gallant, Hedonist, Jester, Leader, Paragon, Rebel, Thrillseeker, Visionary

The Great Curse: A Wood Exalted whose curse
derives from her Compassion will often be one who
focuses on the nurturing aspect of life. When she is
elementally possessed, she will attempt to protect the
things she guides and nurtures. In contrast, one whose
curse derives from her Conviction will likely focus on
“pruning” and become more cold-hearted and cruel as
she carries out her grim tasks. A Temperate Aspect of
Wood is likely given to bouts of hedonism and will either
go on wild binges or feel remorse and regret at her lack of
control. If the Wood-aspected Exalt’s curse derives from
her Valor, she may be in the habit of seeking out extreme
and even dangerous experiences, heedless of whatever
jeopardy she places her life in.

Associations: The color green, the Maiden of Endings, the season of Wood and the eastern direction

Sobriquets: Children of Sextes Jylis, Ashen Bows,
Emerald Dragons

Concepts: Cunning courtier, professional assassin,
Immaculate inquisitor, master healer, meticulous merchant prince

The Terrestrial Exalted’s magic is perhaps less powerful than that of the Anathema, but the children of the Five
Elemental Dragons should not be underestimated. Reduced though they may be, their empire still encompasses
almost the entire civilized world. This section describes

‘the Charms that form the backbone of the Dragon-

Blooded’s arsenal.


### Elemental Attunement


Much as the magic of the Solar Exalted is divided up
between the different castes, Terrestrial Exalted Charms
are divided up along elemental lines. This doesn’t mean
that Dragon-Blooded of a various elemental attunements
can’t learn each other’s Charms. Quite to the contrary, all
living:things contain all of the Five Dragon Elements to
some degree. Controlling an element other than one’s own
is just a bit more difficult, requiring a greater expenditure

The elemental attunement of a Dynast deter-

the 1 mote surcharge once for each Charm of an element
different from her own. The Combo surcharge is payable
immediately upon activation of the Combo.

For Example: A Wood-aspected Dynast uses a Combo
containing Wood, Fire and Water Charms. She must pay
a2 mote surcharge upon activating the Combo; | mote for
Fire Charms, 1 mote for Wood Charms. This is on top of
any costs to activate the individual Charms and the 1
temporary Willpower to activate the Combo.

Members of the Eclipse Caste who learn these Charms
have noelementand, thus, have no Charms for which they
do not pay the surcharge.

### Language-learning Ritual


Cost: 5 motes, | Willpower
Duration: One week

Type: Simple

Minimum Linguistics: 2

Minimum Essence: 1

Prerequisite Charms: None

Dragon-Blooded embarking on diplomatic missions
often invoke this Charm. By spending an hour meditating
and listening to a native speak the language to be learned,
the Exalt absorbs the knowledge of the language into her
mind. The character needs some sort of focus to meditate
on, usually a cultural object related to or a book written in
the language to be learned. For the Charm’s duration, the
Dragon-Blooded is completely fluent and naturally conversant in the language, without even a trace of an accent.
The character may also read and write the language. Once
the duration expires, the knowledge fades immediately.


### Thousand Tongues Meditation


Cost: 4 motes
Duration: One scene
Type: Simple
Minimum Linguistics: 3
Minimum Essence: 2

Prerequisite Charms: Language-Learning Ritual

The far-ranging colonial interests of the Dragon-
Blooded combine with the Air-aspected Exalt’s close
affinity with communication to make this Charm a
common one.

The character speaking in tongues can easily speak
and understand any spoken language for the duration of
the Charm. If speaking to beings each speaking a different
language, the character can understand them all, and they
can all understand the character — but they will still be
unable to understand each other. The maximum number
of beings that an Exalted may speak in tongues to at once
is equal to the:character’s permanent Essence.

This Charm allows an Exalted to “hijack” another’s
conversation. The effect is subtle, but it can be deadly if
applied correctly. The Exalt must be able to hear the
conversation to twist it, but need not actually be present.
Eavesdropping through Charms such as Voices on the
Wind is perfectly permissible, but the range of this
Charm cannot be extended past 100 feet x the Exalt’s
permanent Essence.

To twist'a conversation, the character's player must
roll Manipulation + Linguistics. The number of successes
indicates the degree to which the Dragon-Blooded can
twist the conversation. A single success would allow her to

realizes the conversation is being manipulated and can
indicate this through nonverbal means (normally a chopping motion at one’s own throat). Use of this Charm ina
